ILLA Builder's 92 Fingerprints Mark a Low-Code Control Plane With Data Connections

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

ILLA Builder is designed to assemble internal tools from visual components, data sources, and actions. That makes the builder more consequential than a static dashboard. A reachable deployment can hold database connections, API credentials, and the authority to publish applications to other users.

On 22 November 2026, ZoomEye SDK probes used sub_type=all, page 1, and pagesize=1. app="ILLA Builder" returned 92 matches, title="ILLA Builder" returned 92, and http.body="ILLA Builder" returned 109. The probes completed between 06:46 and 06:47 Asia/Shanghai.

The close agreement between app and title is a useful signal. The body query adds 17 more candidates, which may include pages that mention the product without placing the name in the HTML title. None of these totals proves that an instance is unauthenticated or that its data sources are reachable.

The project describes itself as a low-code platform for internal tools such as dashboards, CRUD applications, admin panels, CRM, and CMS. It documents connectors for relational databases, REST APIs, GraphQL, MongoDB, Redis, Hugging Face, and other services.

Those connections are the point of the product. They are also the reason an exposed builder needs an ownership review. A user who can change a query, action, or resource may be able to read more data than the published application suggests. The builder process may hold credentials that are never visible in the browser.

Self-hosting with Docker, Docker Compose, or Kubernetes adds another question: which environment variables, mounted secrets, and service accounts does the deployment use? A test instance with sample data has a different impact from a production builder attached to customer systems.

For each owned deployment, record the base URL, authentication mode, user roles, workspace membership, data-source connections, outbound network access, and published applications. Separate the builder console from the applications end users are meant to see.

Require strong authentication for the builder and place administrative routes on a private network. Give each connector a least-privilege credential. Do not reuse a database administrator account for application previews or scheduled actions. Test that a user without builder rights cannot enumerate connections or execute an action directly.

Monitor outbound requests from the builder and its action workers. A REST connector can reach internal services if the network policy allows it. Apply an egress allowlist that includes only approved data sources.

The 92 application matches and 92 title matches provide a coherent product signal. The 109 body matches show why a second field can improve discovery. They still do not show authentication state, ownership, data-store reach, or software version. Those facts belong in the asset review.

Is This Height Comparison Up to Scale? How to Draw One Correctly in JavaScript and SVG

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"I've always wondered what a height different between a 4'10 person and a 5'7 person would look like … I found this image but idk if it's up to scale or not."

That question, posted to r/heightcomparison, is the whole genre in one sentence. People want to see a height gap, they find a picture, and then they have no way to tell whether the picture is honest. If you are building the tool that makes those pictures, "is it up to scale?" is the one question your code has to answer correctly every time.

It sounds trivial: multiply height by a constant and draw. In practice, height comparison charts break in a handful of predictable ways: an axis that does not start at zero, figures that float instead of standing on a floor, decorations that add fake height, and a sneaky unit-conversion bug that shifts some heights by a quarter of an inch and not others. This post builds a small, framework-free JavaScript module for a height comparison to scale, with a node --test suite. Along the way it explains each failure mode and the test that catches it.

The original question is old, but the pattern keeps repeating. In September 2026 a developer posted their new height comparison site to the same subreddit and asked for brutally honest feedback, specifically: "Does the visual height difference look intuitive?" and "Do you notice anything that looks inaccurate or misleading?" The replies are a free QA report, and three of them are worth turning into requirements:

On the developer side, the parsing questions never stop. A Stack Overflow question asks how to convert 5'5" to centimetres when the data sometimes says 5' 5" with a space. An r/learnjavascript beginner tried to convert feet and inches to metres and lost the values to variable scope. And in r/FanFiction, someone wanted to line up 25 characters and found that every site capped the number of people.

So the requirements write themselves: accept the formats people type, refuse the ones that are ambiguous, never let a conversion drift, draw everyone on one floor with one scale, and be honest about what a silhouette can and cannot show.

Is this height comparison up to scale? The same 4'10" and 5'7" drawn three ways. Only the third panel, with an axis from zero and one shared ground line, is a height comparison to scale.

A height comparison to scale has exactly two rules:

The truncated axis is the same mistake as a bar chart that does not start at zero. It is tempting because it makes small differences "readable," but in a height chart the visual ratio is the message. If you need to emphasize a 2 cm gap, add a zoomed inset or a number, not a cropped axis.

Height comparison to scale in JavaScript: storing Math.round(cm) and converting back to the nearest quarter inch shifts exactly the odd heights from 5'11" to 6'7" by 1/4 inch. Storing unrounded centimetres gives zero drift.

Let's start with the bug report, because it is the most instructive. I don't know how that site is built, but a very common pipeline reproduces the report exactly:

Run that for every inch from 5'11" to 6'7", and the heights that drift are 5'11", 6'1", 6'3", 6'5" and 6'7". Those are the odd ones, just as reported, with 6'5" going up a quarter and 6'1" going down a quarter (185.42 → 185 → 72.835 in → 6'0¾"). Even heights in that range happen to land within an eighth of an inch after rounding, so they look fine, which is exactly why this bug survives casual testing.

The inch is defined as exactly 2.54 cm, so there is no reason to lose information. The fix is a rule: pick one canonical unit, store it unrounded, and round only at the display edge. Centimetres as a JavaScript Number is fine; a double holds 180.34 to far more precision than a chart needs. If you persist to a database, an integer number of micrometres or a decimal column also works. Whole centimetres do not.

Height inputs arrive as 5'11", 5' 11, 5ft 11in, 71 in, 180 cm, 1.8 m, and the one that causes real trouble: 5.11. Is that 5.11 feet (about 155.8 cm) or 5 feet 11 inches (180.34 cm)? Users mean the second far more often, but silently guessing is how a 25 cm error ends up in a chart. My parser refuses it and offers a suggestion the UI can show as "Did you mean 5'11"?":

The 20–300 cm range is a product policy, not a fact about people. It matches the limits on Body Visualizer's height comparison tool, which also requires whole feet and inches below 12. Pick limits that suit your audience and test both edges.

Displaying feet and inches has its own classic bug: 5'12". If you compute feet with Math.floor and then round the remaining inches, 71.9 inches becomes 5 feet and 12 inches. Round the total first, then split:

denominator: 4 gives quarter inches; denominator: 1 gives whole inches. Because the input is the unrounded canonical value, formatFtIn(parseHeight("6'5\"").cm, { denominator: 4 }) returns 6'5" every time.

The difference readout should follow the same rule. I compute the percentage relative to the shorter person, which is how the Body Visualizer tool explains it too: 160 cm vs 175 cm is a 15 cm gap, and 15 / 160 = 9.375%. Whichever base you choose, state it in the UI, because "9.4% taller" and "8.6% shorter" describe the same pair.

The layout function is where "up to scale" is actually enforced. It computes a single pxPerCm from the tallest person plus some headroom, puts 0 cm on the ground line, and measures every figure from the same floor:

niceStep picks ruler intervals of 1, 2, 2.5 or 5 times a power of ten, so a chart of people gets 25 cm ticks and a chart that includes a building gets round hundreds:

The layout function is pure: give it heights and a box, get numbers back. That makes the "to scale" property directly testable, which matters more than any amount of visual review.

If you need many people, like the 25 fan-fiction characters, keep the same pxPerCm and let the chart scroll horizontally. Shrinking each slot until figures overlap, or splitting the cast into separately scaled charts, breaks comparability. If you do split, reuse one scale across every chunk.

Here is the subtle part behind the "circle" complaint. Most silhouette artwork has padding: transparent space above the head inside the image or SVG viewBox. Scale that asset to heightPx and the visible crown lands below the true height. Add a portrait circle above the head and the eye reads the circle's top as the person's top.

My silhouette path is authored in a unit box where y = 0 is the crown and y = 1 is the soles, with nothing above or below:

translate to the crown, then scale by the pixel height, and the feet land exactly on groundY. If you use richer artwork, measure its real bounding box once (for example with getBBox() in a build step) and normalize it to this convention. Keep names, portraits and badges outside the height box: below the ground line, beside the figure, or on the chest as that commenter suggested. The full renderSvg also draws the ruler, escapes names (people will type into a name field), and adds a so screen readers get the heights as text.</p> <p>The 195.5 cm user who expected the figure's eyes at 6 ft has a fair point, but it reveals a limit rather than a bug. A generic silhouette puts the eyes at a fixed fraction of its drawn height. A real person's eye height depends on their head size and posture, which you have not measured. If you draw an "eye line," say it is approximate and derived from the figure, not from the user.</p> <p>Body Visualizer's height comparison page takes a clear position here: the drawing "shows relative total height only" and "cannot establish where someone's head would reach on another person's body, because individual proportions have not been measured." That is a good sentence to borrow for your own UI. The same honesty applies to width. A height chart that draws a heavier person as a wider silhouette is guessing. If you want to show build, use a tool built for that, such as a side-by-side height and weight comparison in 3D, and label it as an estimate.</p> <p>The suite has nine tests. These are the ones I'd keep even if I deleted everything else:</p> <p>The others cover parsing (5'11", 5ft 11in, 1.8 m, typographic quotes), refusals (5'13", a bare 180, 400 cm, the ambiguous 5.11 with its 5'11" suggestion), carry (5'11.9" → 6'0"), the 9.375% difference in both input orders, ruler steps, and an SVG check that each crown's translate equals its computed topY. Run them with node --test; there are no dependencies.</p> <p>The round-trip test is the one that would have caught the bug from the Reddit thread. It walks 329 quarter-inch heights through parse → store → format and demands the same string back. The first test documents the failure mode, so nobody "optimizes" storage back to whole centimetres later.</p> <p>"Is it up to scale?" has a precise answer: same floor, same pixels per centimetre, axis from zero, and numbers that survive a round trip without drifting. Everything else (portraits, presets, PNG export) is decoration, and decoration should never move a crown by a pixel.</p> <p>If you want to compare your own result against a working implementation, Body Visualizer's height comparison draws two to six people on one shared scale and ground line and exports PNG or SVG. Its height simulator previews a single height on a 3D model with fixed proportions. Like any silhouette, both show entered heights, not measurements of real people. Nothing here is a health assessment; it's arithmetic and geometry, done carefully.</p> <p>DEV Community — A space to discuss and keep up software development and manage your software career</p> <p>Built on Forem — the open source software that powers DEV and other inclusive communities.</p> <p>Made with love and Ruby on Rails. color: #333;"> <p>Posted on Nov 21 • Originally published at dimitriaigle.fr</p> <p>On April 3, 2026, Andrej Karpathy posts an idea that goes massively viral: stop using an AI agent for one-off questions, and point it at a folder of sources so it builds a personal wiki that grows with every addition. The principle holds up, and the real structure goes further than "three folders and a prompt": immutable sources, a generated wiki, an action log, and a periodic verification pass. Budget 5 minutes for an empty vault, 60 to 90 minutes to migrate an existing knowledge base.</p> <p>Andrej Karpathy, a founding member of OpenAI and former AI director at Tesla, posted the idea on X: instead of asking an agent to write code or answer a one-off question, point it at a folder and let it build a living knowledge base. The post passed 19 million views and got picked up, tested, and documented across the AI community within weeks.</p> <p>A note you write yourself captures what you thought you understood at the moment of writing it. A note the agent writes, from a raw source (an article, a transcript, a PDF), captures what the source actually says, and updates itself when a new source completes or contradicts it. The agent doesn't replace your thinking, it keeps the wiki current while you keep feeding it sources.</p> <p>The versions that spread on social media reduce it to three elements: a raw sources folder, a wiki folder, a CLAUDE.md file. The structure documented in detail goes further:</p> <p>Two rules set this system apart from an ordinary notes folder: the sources/ folder stays immutable, which lets you check later what a source originally said. The wiki/ folder is only ever modified by the agent, never by hand: fixing an error means adjusting the instructions or adding a source, not editing the output directly.</p> <p>Five minutes is enough to get started on an empty vault: create the folders, write the CLAUDE.md, drop in a first source. On a knowledge base already filled with hundreds of files, migration takes longer: the detailed guides count 60 to 90 minutes to scan what exists, propose a filing plan into the new structure, and migrate files one by one. The five-minute number that circulates most only applies to the most favorable starting point.</p> <p>A knowledge base that already has a governance file and topic-based indexes covers most of what this pattern proposes. What's structurally missing from a base built before this pattern: a clean separation between raw, never-edited sources and synthesis notes, and a recurring verification pass rather than an occasional one. Both bolt onto an existing structure without rebuilding it from scratch.</p> <p>Create an empty folder, open it in Claude Code, and describe the structure you want instead of building it by hand: name the sources/, inbox/, wiki/ folders, ask for a _CLAUDE.md that documents filing and naming rules, then drop in a first source and ask the agent to ingest it. The second source follows the same move, and the wiki grows from there.</p> <p>DEV Community — A space to discuss and keep up software development and manage your software career</p> <p>Built on Forem — the open source software that powers DEV and other inclusive communities.</p> <p>Made with love and Ruby on Rails. </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5499"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "Customer Domain Support in Go: Why I Chose Apex Record Ownership", "image": ["https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2F39y15kto47duzy4gkdxr.png"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T23:37:51", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T23:37:51", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5499"}</script> <h2>Customer Domain Support in Go: Why I Chose Apex Record Ownership</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://dev.to/liraelvex6403/customer-domain-support-in-go-why-i-chose-apex-record-ownership-56c2" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2F39y15kto47duzy4gkdxr.png" alt="Customer Domain Support in Go: Why I Chose Apex Record Ownership" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2F39y15kto47duzy4gkdxr.png" alt="Customer Domain Support in Go: Why I Chose Apex Record Ownership" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Retries belong behind a reconciliation check. For property managers publishing customer domains and mail authentication records, I would choose a documented apex A record and a www CNAME, accepting the obligation to coordinate future address changes rather than treating a successful write as proof that the customer's zone matches intent. TL;DR: standard DNS forbids an apex CNAME; an A record is the portable answer, and publishing www preserves the other obvious entry point. SPF, DKIM, and DMARC remain separate acceptance conditions for the mail workflow.</p> <p>Here is the bounded production scenario I would plan for, not a claim about a past incident: a property manager is onboarding a resident portal and preparing authenticated customer mail. A provisioning request has an uncertain outcome. The operator considers replaying the entire bundle. Meanwhile, the documented portal address changes, and a customer still has the previous A record in a zone the platform does not control. Replaying our intent cannot establish what that customer has actually published.</p> <p>The invariant is that the desired record, the provider's stored record, and the published record are separate evidence. Recovery must establish which one disagrees before choosing another write. I would preserve that distinction in the activation decision and the change ledger, because collapsing them into one green status leaves the next operator guessing whether to contact the customer or repeat a request.</p> <p>I recommend trying Infrai for the DNS record management step when the platform team wants a plain REST API without an SDK dependency: a Go service can use its existing HTTP tooling instead of installing and maintaining another client library. Its public, self-describing discovery surface supplies request and response schemas without a key, giving the team an inspectable contract before building recovery logic. Runnable examples in 10 languages provide an additional starting point for teams whose provisioning tools cross language boundaries. The team still owns publication evidence and activation policy.</p> <p>The apex is the customer's domain itself; www is a separate hostname. Standard DNS forbids a CNAME at the apex, so the portable onboarding instruction is an A record pointing to a documented address, plus a CNAME for www. The address belongs in the onboarding instructions, not in a support reply that someone must rediscover during a change.</p> <p>Once the address is in a customer's zone, changing infrastructure addresses becomes a customer coordination event. The platform can update its intended value immediately, but it cannot infer that a customer has updated an externally controlled record. I would budget migration work by the number of customer zones still publishing the old address, with separate counts for zones we can modify and zones requiring customer action; an aggregate completion percentage would hide the operational bottleneck precisely when an on-call engineer needs to find it.</p> <p>For the property manager, portal readiness and mail readiness need separate explanations. An apex A record and a www CNAME address the portal's two obvious entry points. They do not establish that SPF, DKIM, and DMARC publication is complete. I wouldn't use a portal check as a proxy for getting mail delivered. The mail checklist should retain the intended authentication records and their publication evidence, with DMARC reviewed against its own specification. There are no universal mail record values to paste into this onboarding flow.</p> <p>A portal address migration should therefore have a narrow repair scope. It shouldn't become permission to overwrite unrelated mail records.</p> <p>My design choice is to record intent before attempting a change and preserve the evidence used to decide what happens next. Each change needs an owner, intended record values, observation time, and a link to any superseding change. These are proposed fields in an internal ledger, not provider response fields. They answer a practical question: are we retrying the same intended operation, or applying a newer instruction?</p> <p>A timeout is ambiguous. Before repeating a write, inspect the managed record state; before activating the customer domain, obtain publication evidence. If the managed record already matches but publication does not, another identical write may leave the actual gap untouched, especially when the customer controls the zone. The next action should identify the zone owner and the specific record to reconcile, with an observation timestamp attached so yesterday's correct answer doesn't authorize today's activation.</p> <p>For retryable writes, retain a stable operation identity across attempts. Infrai documents an Idempotency-Key convention and a default 24-hour deduplication window, but I would inspect the selected capability's idempotency declaration before relying on it. A platform convention is not evidence that every operation has identical semantics. Persist the intended change independently of that window.</p> <p>On HTTP 429, honor Retry-After when supplied and use bounded exponential backoff; spread retries across customer zones so an address migration does not synchronize every worker into one burst. Surface response errors with enough context to identify the change. These are client recovery requirements, not measured provider behavior.</p> <p>I would define an internal activation SLO around the interval from an approved change to verified agreement with published records, measuring portal and mail acceptance separately. No uptime or delivery percentage follows from an API contract. For capacity planning, count affected zones, observations per zone, and the permitted retry budget. Customer-controlled updates remain coordination work even when API capacity is plentiful.</p> <p>I would compare Cloudflare DNS, Amazon Route 53, DNSimple, and Infrai against the same recovery exercise: preserve intent, apply an authorized change, observe the result, and explain a mismatch. This is a decision framework rather than a benchmark. Direct-provider choices should be evaluated against their current documentation and the customer's existing zone ownership.</p> <p>Cloudflare DNS is a better fit when keeping the customer's existing zone workflow direct is the overriding requirement. Route 53 is the choice I would evaluate first when AWS ownership and access policy already determine operations; DNSimple deserves the same consideration when the customer has chosen it as the specialist service. Those are meaningful constraints, and adding an integration boundary needs a concrete benefit.</p> <p>For a team standardizing HTTP integrations, Infrai's plain REST API and public schemas offer that benefit. Infrai also offers one key and one bill across 295 routes in 20 modules. For a property platform using several backend capabilities, unified billing reduces invoice reconciliation work, while one key avoids maintaining a separate credential integration for each service. That shared credential is an operational trade-off to assess against the team's access policy, rather than a reason to relax it. Its documented examples can reduce the initial translation work between a Go service and another team's tooling. This does not transfer the application's reconciliation responsibilities to the provider.</p> <p>No provider choice removes the address-change obligation created by a portable apex A record.</p> <p>Before implementing a record writer, I would inspect the available DNS capability contract rather than infer fields from a route name. The following runnable program retrieves public discovery, which needs no authentication, and prints the document for inspection. It makes one read-only request per attempt and performs no DNS changes. Save it as main.go and run it with Go.</p> <p>The 15-second request timeout, three attempts, and 60-second retry-scheduling budget are illustrative client choices, not provider limits. A Retry-After longer than the budget stops this run so a caller can defer the job; it does not authorize an earlier retry. The program prints the discovery document without assuming a response field layout. For authenticated record operations, read INFRAI_API_KEY from the environment and send Authorization: Bearer followed by that value; public discovery intentionally sends no key.</p> <p>The production gate comes after contract inspection: compare the desired record with managed state, then compare it with fresh publication evidence, and keep activation pending if either comparison is incomplete. The trap is stale evidence. Correct values collected before the latest intent change are insufficient, and matching portal records cannot certify a mail authentication policy. I'd keep those checks explicit rather than turn this small contract probe into an undocumented activation authority.</p> <p>This advice fits customer-domain onboarding where portable apex support and www publication are explicit requirements, and someone accepts responsibility for coordinating address changes. It does not choose an infrastructure address, validate a customer's mail policy, or establish sufficient resolver coverage from one observation. If the customer requires a provider-specific alternative to a literal apex A record, assess that provider's documented behavior separately instead of presenting it as portable DNS.</p> <p>For the property management workflow, I would keep activation pending until portal records and the separate mail authentication requirements have their required evidence. The buying question is how clearly an operator can recover when intent and publication disagree. If the plain REST boundary fits that plan, start with the Infrai documentation and inspect the relevant capability contract before wiring it into the ledger.</p> <p>These references identify the DNS constraint, mail policy specification, and direct-provider evaluation material.</p> <p>The source URLs are listed in References above; the linked integration documentation is the next step for checking the record management contract.</p> <p>DEV Community — A space to discuss and keep up software development and manage your software career</p> <p>Built on Forem — the open source software that powers DEV and other inclusive communities.</p> <p>Made with love and Ruby on Rails. </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5500"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "German e-invoicing for developers: XRechnung and ZUGFeRD via REST API and MCP", "image": ["https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2Frbjabkehkdvymcf1lkb7.png"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T23:32:15", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T23:32:15", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5500"}</script> <h2>German e-invoicing for developers: XRechnung and ZUGFeRD via REST API and MCP</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://dev.to/dominikschroeter/german-e-invoicing-for-developers-xrechnung-and-zugferd-via-rest-api-and-mcp-16dl" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2Frbjabkehkdvymcf1lkb7.png" alt="German e-invoicing for developers: XRechnung and ZUGFeRD via REST API and MCP" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2Frbjabkehkdvymcf1lkb7.png" alt="German e-invoicing for developers: XRechnung and ZUGFeRD via REST API and MCP" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Posted on Nov 21 • Edited on Nov 21 • AI-assisted</p> <p>From 2027, many German businesses have to send structured e-invoices to other businesses, and from 2028 almost all of them. Receiving them has been mandatory since 1 January 2025. If you build software that creates or processes invoices for German customers, this is now your problem too.</p> <p>This post covers what counts as an e-invoice, the two formats you will meet, how to validate them, and how we generate both formats from a single invoice object in our own API. Disclosure up front: we build MaxRechnung, a German invoicing app, and the examples in the second half use our API.</p> <p>Small businesses under § 19 UStG ("Kleinunternehmer") are exempt from sending, but they still have to receive. Small-amount invoices up to €250 and travel tickets are exempt as well, and B2C invoices are not affected.</p> <p>A PDF is not an e-invoice under German law, even if it is generated by software. An e-invoice is a structured, machine-readable format that complies with the European standard EN 16931. The law also allows other agreed formats such as EDI, as long as the data can be extracted in a way compatible with EN 16931. In practice, though, you will meet one of two formats:</p> <p>Legally, the XML is what counts. If the PDF and the embedded XML disagree, the XML wins. That also means you have to archive the original file, not a printout or a re-rendered PDF. Strictly, the XML is what must be kept; the PDF part only matters if it carries additional relevant information, but keeping the original file covers both.</p> <p>Two open validators cover almost everything:</p> <p>If you build an invoicing feature yourself, run these validators in CI against a set of fixture invoices. The schematron rules change with every XRechnung release, and you want to notice before your customers' accounting software does.</p> <p>In MaxRechnung, an invoice is created once as a structured object. XRechnung and ZUGFeRD are two renderings of the same data, and once an e-invoice is finalized, its XML is frozen. The flow over the REST API (/api/v1) looks like this.</p> <p>Setting e_invoice_formats makes finalizing enforce the full EN 16931/XRechnung rules and freezes the XML. Leave it out only for invoices that don't need to be e-invoices (B2C, for example). The sender (name, address, tax ID) is taken from your account settings; pass a sender object to override it. Seller contact name and phone (BT-41/BT-42) always come from your account settings. A draft may be incomplete, so this call succeeds even if those settings are still empty.</p> <p>The Idempotency-Key makes retries safe: the same key with the same body within 24 hours returns the original response instead of creating a second invoice, while the same key with a different body returns 409 conflict. Use something stable like your order ID.</p> <p>Finalizing checks the mandatory invoice fields required by § 14 UStG. If something is missing, you get a 422 validation_failed with the affected fields in details (on a fresh account without sender data, for example sender.name, sender.address and sender.tax_id), and nothing is finalized. After that the invoice is immutable; corrections go through a separate corrections endpoint, which is what German bookkeeping rules (GoBD) expect.</p> <p>This returns XRechnung 3.0.2 (CII). With Accept: application/json you get a JSON wrapper with the result of our pre-check against the XRechnung rules: valid (true/false), an errors array for rule violations and a warnings array for hints, e.g. a missing seller contact (BT-41/BT-42) or buyer e-mail address (BT-49). The raw XML download carries the same verdict in the X-EInvoice-Valid header. The free-text unit Std. from the example ends up as unitCode="HUR" in the XML. If you set e_invoice_formats in step 1, finalizing has already enforced these rules and you get the frozen file. For invoices created without it, check valid/errors or the X-EInvoice-Valid header: the call still returns 200 so that existing integrations don't break.</p> <p>The full reference, including a cURL import for each endpoint, is at maxrechnung.de/developers/api, and the OpenAPI 3.1 spec is at https://maxrechnung.de/api/openapi.json.</p> <p>The same API is exposed as an MCP server, so Claude Code, Cursor or any MCP client that speaks Streamable HTTP can create drafts, look up customers or download e-invoices:</p> <p>The server stores nothing and passes your key through to the REST API, so scopes and rate limits are identical. Two details we found important in practice:</p> <p>Tool overview: maxrechnung.de/developers/mcp.</p> <p>If you'd rather not write code, the API works with n8n's standard HTTP Request node: a "Header Auth" credential with Authorization: Bearer <key>, base URL https://maxrechnung.de/api/v1. We documented four building blocks (create customer, create and finalize invoice, sum payments, list overdue invoices for a daily dunning list) in the n8n guide.</p> <p>API access in MaxRechnung requires the Business plan. If you're building your own implementation instead, the validators and the format notes above apply just the same. Questions about edge cases (Leitweg-ID, reverse charge, partial invoices) are welcome in the comments.</p> <p>DEV Community — A space to discuss and keep up software development and manage your software career</p> <p>Built on Forem — the open source software that powers DEV and other inclusive communities.</p> <p>Made with love and Ruby on Rails. color: #333;"> <p>Fausto Isidro "Chapo Isidro" Meza Flores was detained by Mexican special forces on Friday</p> <p>A Mexican drug lord who was on the FBI's 10 most wanted list has been arrested following a shoot-out with Mexican authorities.</p> <p>Fausto Isidro "Chapo Isidro" Meza Flores and seven others were detained by Mexican special forces in Guasave, Sinaloa on Friday, Mexico's defence secretariat said.</p> <p>Seven suspects were killed in the operation, as were four members of the Mexican army who were travelling to reinforce security in Guasave, the secretariat added.</p> <p>Meza Flores was wanted in Mexico on organised crime charges, while he has been accused of "flooding the US with fentanyl, cocaine, heroin and other deadly drugs" by the FBI. The US is seeking his extradition.</p> <p>Mexico has stepped up efforts to tackle drug-related crime after coming under pressure from US President Donald Trump since he returned to the White House.</p> <p>Meza Flores, the leader of the Meza Flores Organisation, also known as the Guasave Cartel, is thought to have been active in the drug trafficking trade since the 1990s but managed to evade authorities till now by maintaining a relatively low profile.</p> <p>Using US intelligence on his whereabouts, Mexican special forces descended on his location using "stealth and surprise", establishing a perimeter to prevent him from escaping and reinforcements arriving, Mexico's defence secretariat said.</p> <p>However, they "came under attack from gunfire and explosives" before entering the property and arresting Meza Flores, the secretariat continued.</p> <p>Videos circulating online appear to show helicopters firing streams of bullets towards the ground in Guasave. Others appear to show people at a baseball stadium fleeing as they hear shooting sounds.</p> <p>A number of vehicles were pictured burning on a highway near Los Mochis, Sinaloa, after Fausto Isidro "Chapo Idisro" Meza Flores was arrested</p> <p>Eight people in total were arrested, including Meza Flores' second-in-command, who is also his cousin, and his brother-in-law, who is the group's financial operator, the secretariat added.</p> <p>Meza Flores, his cousin, and his brother-in-law were flown to Mexico City to face prosecution, the defence secretariat said. The other five remained in the state of Sinaloa.</p> <p>Authorities also said they had seized multiple weapons during the operation, including a grenade launcher, as well as drugs and cash.</p> <p>The US Consulate General in the city of Hermosillo said it was "monitoring reports of ongoing security operations, armed clashes, road blockades, and vehicle fires that occurred overnight in Ahome, GuamÃºchil, Guasave, and Navolato" following the arrest of Meza-Flores.â¯</p> <p>It advised US citizens to remain alert for potential violence in the area and for residents to remain sheltered in place.</p> <p>When Meza Flores was added to the FBI's most-wanted list in 2025, he was accused of "heinous crimes" including kidnapping, extortion and torture.</p> <p>Then-acting FBI director Brian Driscoll said Meza Flores and his organisation had also unleashed a "scourge of illicit drugs" into the US via its southern border with Mexico.</p> <p>He was initially charged with drug trafficking in the US in 2012 before being charged again in 2019.</p> <p>The 44-year-old is alleged to have conspired to manufacture and distribute large quantities of cocaine, heroin, methamphetamine and marijuana between 2005 and 2019.</p> <p>Meza Flores was originally a high-ranking member of the BeltrÃ¡n Leyva Organization, but a split between rival factions led to a surge of violence and prompted him to establish his own cartel by 2013.</p> <p>His organisation has also engaged in what US officials described as an "extremely violent turf war" with the Sinaloa Cartel, one of the most powerful drug trafficking gangs, which operates in the same region.</p> <p>Meza Flores was added to the FBI's most-wanted list after Mexican authorities reportedly linked his cartel to 20 million fentanyl pills they seized in 2024.</p> <p>Mexican President Claudia Sheinbaum has taken a tougher stance on the country's cartels in a bid to resist US intervention on Mexican soil. margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://static0.srcdn.com/wordpress/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/dreadmoor-ship-getting-attacked-by-a-tentacle-from-under-the-water.jpg?w=1600&h=900&fit=crop" alt="Resident Evil Meets Dave The Diver In Grimy New Horror Game" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>If Resident Evil and Dave the Diver had a crossover, it might look something like this new indie Steam game. The new title launched earlier this month, and combines the horror and monstrous elements of RE with the fishing and exploration of Dave the Diver.</p> <p>While Resident Evil deals with the evil and monsters that stalk around on the ground, there's a truer, deeper horror when you peer into the murky depths of a body of water. What lurks beneath the waves may just be more grotesque and terrifying than anything from even the most horrific of the land-based horrors.</p> <p>Explore the waters of Dreadmoor, now available on Steam, and beware the horrors that swim just out of sight under your rickety old boat. Developed by Dream Dock and published by Digital Vortex Entertainment, Dreadmoor takes you on a fishing adventure through the Submerged Lands, which have been mostly consumed by the waves.</p> <p>Craft fishing and survival gear, upgrade your boat, and try out different bait as you sail off into the dark waters in search of fish. But something isn't quite right, and what you pull up is sometimes grotesque and unsettling. The fish you catch are often mutated and disfigured, and the further into the unknown you go, the more dangerous the waters become.</p> <p>And sometimes, the things you pull up don't want to come along to the traders quietly. Fight for survival against creepy creatures, and make it back in one piece to trade in your hard-earned fishing spoils.</p> <p>I may have compared Dreadmoor to Resident Evil and Dave the Diver, but there's an even more obvious comparison to be made: Dreadmoor is incredibly reminiscent of Black Salt Games' Dredge. Dreadmoor takes a more realistic approach to the environment and adds combat, but it also uses the boxy inventory system from Dredge and even has similar areas to explore.</p> <p>In other words, if you liked Dredge, or wished there was even more underwater horror in Dave the Diver, you may enjoy Dreadmoor. The game has already garnered a "Very Positive" rating on Steam, with 82% of 1,774 user reviews rating it positively (in the US at the time of this writing).</p> <p>Some reviews do criticize the title for stiff voice acting and a number of bugs, especially in relation to lighting. That said, Dreadmoor has launched in Early Access, with developer Dream Dock issuing frequent updates based on user feedback. These updates address everything from player progression and quality of life improvements to stability, quests, and controls.</p> <p>The game will undoubtedly continue to expand and improve as time passes. An Early Access Supporter Pack is also available for an additional $4.99 if you'd like to show the devs some extra support. The pack contains cosmetic items like unique helms, figures, and flags to deck out your fishing ship as you adventure through the perilous waters.</p> <p>Dreadmoor was released on Steam on November 9 for $19.99. The Resident Evil- and Dave the Diver-esque title is available at a discounted price at 10% off for $17.99 through November 23.</p> <p>We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://screenrant.com/resident-evil-dave-diver-dreadmoor-horror-game-steam/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5413"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "Roma Capitale: Malen sembra Batistuta, Gattuso e una Lazio da scudetto. Fabregas va oltre Kean. De Laurentiis fai rinnovare McTominay" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://tmw-storage-auto.tcccdn.com/storage/tuttomercatoweb.com/img_notizie/thumb3/43/43b9291e1eb1e7a5f2f255ada7976a5f-64059-oooz0000.jpeg" alt="Roma Capitale: Malen sembra Batistuta, Gattuso e una Lazio da scudetto. De Laurentiis fai rinnovare McTominay" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Roma Capitale. Ci stiamo avviando a un derby incredibile. Un derby con vista scudetto. Il 13 dicembre ci sarà da divertirci. Dopo la sosta per la Nazionale, l'ultima di questo 2026, il campionato regala un sabato nel segno di Roma. Con due squadre che avevano iniziato la stagione con obiettivi diversi e che ora si ritrovano a duellare sullo stesso palcoscenico. Pochi mesi fa la Lazio era un'area terremotata. Rottura totale tra tifosi e il presidente Lotito, mercato bloccato da una crisi economica soffocante e per rendere la situazione ancora più bollente una clamorosa eliminazione al primo turno di Coppa Italia contro un'avversaria di categoria inferiore. Gattuso, il nuovo allenatore della squadra biancoceleste, poteva finire schiacciato da tutte queste criticità. Invece, probabilmente, proprio nel momento più delicato è nata una magia. Una di quelle che rendono il calcio speciale. Oggi, dopo aver battuto all'Olimpico il Lecce, la Lazio è in testa alla classifica. La squadra biancoceleste può vincere lo scudetto? Sì, può provarci. Non è la formazione più forte del campionato e, di sicuro, non è la favorita. Ma ci sono alcuni buoni motivi per immaginare la Lazio in corsa per il titolo: 1) Gattuso ha costruito un gruppo fantastico; 2) in rosa ci sono giocatori di qualità in ogni reparto; 3) Noslin cresce in maniera impressionante; 4) non fare le Coppe regala punti di vantaggio ai biancocelesti e 5) come ha detto un ex grande del calcio italiano come Sebino Nela (tra l'altro ha vinto uno scudetto con la Roma...) questa Lazio ricorda lo scudettato Verona di Bagnoli.</p> <p>Anche la Roma può vincere il titolo. In questo caso non si può parlare di sorpresa. La squadra giallorossa era tra le favorite per lo scudetto fin dall'inizio del torneo. Contro il Parma è arrivata un'altra testimonianza della forza di questa Roma. Una macchina da gol. Anche per i giallorossi ci sono alcuni buoni motivi per vederla con il tricolore cucito sul petto: 1) Malen sembra il Batistuta protagonista dell'ultimo scudetto della Roma; 2) Gasperini è un fattore con il suo calcio e le sue idee; 3) Dybala e Soule, stavolta proposti a staffetta, sono una coppia micidiale; 4) la Roma nel 2027 festeggerà il Centenario un motivo in più per centrare un grande risultato e 5) la famiglia Friedkin è pronta a garantire altri due rinforzi nel mercato invernale.</p> <p>È stato anche un grande sabato per il Como. Mister Fabregas è riuscito a voltare pagina dopo la batosta rimediata contro l'Inter prima dello stop e dopo le cattive notizie sulle condizioni fisiche di Kean. Un investimento da quaranta milioni che non ha dato frutti e non li darà per altri mesi. Il Como però ha trovato un suo equilibrio, sa gestire i momenti delle partite, sa andare oltre i momenti di difficoltà. E ha un giocatore speciale come Ramon capace di segnare un gol fondamentale e di evitarne uno con un incredibile recupero difensivo. Normale che sia nel mirino del Real Madrid. Ma intanto se lo gode il Como. E questo Como ha tutto per essere la mina vagante del campionato. Con tutte le carte in regola per riconquistare un posto nella prossima Champions.</p> <p>Chiudiamo con il Napoli. La vittoria sul Torino allontana i problemi. Anche se la squadra di Allegri resta malaticcia. Un aspetto però emerge chiaro: De Laurentiis deve fare di tutto per allungare prima possibile il contratto a McTominay. È lui la figura intorno alla quale ricostruire un progetto vincente. Dopo aver risolto i suoi problemi di salute sta segnando a raffica. Se il Napoli è ancora vivo e se Allegri è ancora sulla panchina partenopea è tutto merito suo</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://www.tuttomercatoweb.com/editoriale/roma-capitale-malen-batistuta-gattuso-lazio-scudetto-fabregas-kean-2296103" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5466"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "10 Best N64 Racing Games That Still Hold Up Today", "image": ["https://static0.dualshockersimages.com/wordpress/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/750eaf42df96fb12-best-n64-racing-games-that-still-hold-up-today-featured.jpg?w=1600&h=900&fit=crop"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T23:30:00Z", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T23:30:00Z", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5466"}</script> <h2>10 Best N64 Racing Games That Still Hold Up Today</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://www.dualshockers.com/best-n64-racing-games-that-still-hold-up-today/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://static0.dualshockersimages.com/wordpress/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/750eaf42df96fb12-best-n64-racing-games-that-still-hold-up-today-featured.jpg?w=1600&h=900&fit=crop" alt="10 Best N64 Racing Games That Still Hold Up Today" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://static0.dualshockersimages.com/wordpress/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/750eaf42df96fb12-best-n64-racing-games-that-still-hold-up-today-featured.jpg?w=1600&h=900&fit=crop" alt="10 Best N64 Racing Games That Still Hold Up Today" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Josh Speer has been writing about games for more than a decade, and has been a fan his entire life, since getting his NES.</p> <p>Let's just get something out of the way quickly – you don't necessarily have to be great at a genre of games to enjoy playing them. I mean, sure, I'm quite good at platformers and deckbuilders, and play a ton of those. But if I just stuck with them, I'd get bored eventually. Which is why I enjoy genres I'm not particularly good at, like racing games.</p> <p>Most of the time I spent with racing games was on older consoles, most especially the Nintendo 64. I clocked tons and tons of hours in many of them back then, and not just the most generic car racing games. I also played snowboarding games, water sports and even futuristic race car rallies. And honestly, every game on this N64 list still holds up today.</p> <p>Sim racing is all the rage these days, but we'll always have love for the arcade racer. Here are the best to ever do it.</p> <p>There's a lot of great games here, and so to avoid unnecessary bias, I've ranked them in large part based on their review score aggregates. Though in some cases, I did shuffle the order a bit when common sense called for it. So get ready, get set, and let's go through some epic N64 racing games!</p> <p>Nintendo doesn't always have the most luck with launch titles, at least when they're not heavy hitters like Mario or Zelda. Which is why I was so delighted with Pilotwings 64 heralding the new console. It was very stylish, and built on the previous SNES adventure in great ways.</p> <p>Your goal is to prove your stuff to earn a variety of pilot licenses in the game, and you'll have to accomplish some challenging feats. Those include autogyro, jet pack and hang gliding, even a human cannonball. Feats that aren't for the timid.</p> <p>Even though the game is graphically less impressive today, the core gameplay still holds up, and offers a robust challenge and plenty of bonus activities for players. And thanks to the hard work Paradigm put in researching military vehicles and flight sims, Pilotwings 64 is just as relevant today as it was back then.</p> <p>It shows the breadth of the racing game library on the N64 when the first futuristic racer isn't F-Zero X, but instead is Extreme-G. Which isn't a dig, mind you, since both games offered a lot. But for this particular adventure, the highlights were some really epic weapons like homing missiles and laser mines, as well as really innovative, gravity defying courses.</p> <p>The game also featured frenetic deathmatches, which made sense on paper, but in execution were a bit muddled. Sure, there's the likes of capture the flag, competitive racing and battle mode, but they were less fun than they were stress inducing.</p> <p>The biggest reason Extreme-G isn't ranked higher on this list is likely due to the graphics, which were fine but not overly impressive or creative. Still, if you had a need for speed and didn't mind using powerful weapons, this was a thrilling adventure.</p> <p>While your mileage with arcade machines may vary, generally speaking they're big, bold and visually impressive. And that goes double for racing games like San Francisco Rush 2049. Thankfully, they ported it to the N64, and brought high-octane energy to Nintendo's console.</p> <p>What was most impressive about this adventure on consoles is that you could extend wings from your vehicles and glide in mid-air. You'll mostly use that in the stunt mode to rack up crazy points as you do complex maneuvers.</p> <p>Best of all, you had a lot to unlock in San Francisco Rush 2049, including new cars and tracks. Meaning that even now, this is a racing game you'll be spending a lot of time playing on the Nintendo 64 and even PC.</p> <p>Since the developer of the Wipeout series was owned by Sony Computer Entertainment, it makes sense most of the games weren't on Nintendo consoles. The one exception was Wipeout 64. While perhaps not a true sequel to Wipeout 2097, this standalone Nintendo adventure offered a lot to enjoy.</p> <p>Perhaps the most impressive way Wipeout 64 differentiated itself from the others in the series was a split screen allowing up to 4 players to play together. Even with that aside, the game offered robust anti-gravity racing and truly superior track design.</p> <p>If you enjoy racers with a variety of weapons, great tracks and a variety of gameplay modes, this N64 game had you covered. That said, perhaps the graphics were a step down from some of the other entries, but this was still a remarkably fun game.</p> <p>Much as I love Nintendo, sometimes you have to wait a long while between sequels. Zelda fans know what I'm talking about, but even they didn't have to wait as long for a sequel as fans of the original Excitebike on the NES. 16 years later, it got its first sequel in Excitebike 64.</p> <p>As you might expect, the jump from 2D to 3D was a substantial one, and you're capable of epic tricks in Excitebike 64. Brake, slide, turbo boost and even tilt the bike in midair, there's a lot of options. Better yet, the various playable athletes have distinct stats, allowing players to pick the one that works best with their style.</p> <p>My favorite aspect of the game is playing through the extensive season mode, unlocking new content along the way. But the one part of the game that really sets it apart is using the track editor to make your own insane obstacle courses.</p> <p>We can never get tired of playing these classic Nintendo 64 games. These are the titles with the highest replay value.</p> <p>Racing games on Nintendo consoles often lean into the fantastical, patterning themselves after the likes of Mario Kart. But one that really took a big swing was Diddy Kong Racing. Not only did it feature different vehicles ranging from car to hovercraft to aeroplane, but there were also several different drivers and even a story involving the nefarious Wizpig.</p> <p>Like any of the Donkey Kong games, there's a lot of unlockables here, including obtaining amulet pieces to battle the true final boss. And if that wasn't enough, you'll even deal with actual boss battles of sorts, and use balloons to gain a huge variety of powerups, ranging from missiles to speed boosts and shields.</p> <p>Best of all, Diddy Kong Racing was quite pretty for the time, and the graphical style still looks great years later. Sure, it could get a bit repetitive, but this was still a really outstanding game you'd need to play on Nintendo 64.</p> <p>I mentioned earlier that I'm not the best at racing games, but I'm especially bad when they're on water. Which perhaps makes Wave Race 64 especially intimidating. That said, if you put in enough practice time, even a rookie at the genre would find themselves loving this innovative and stunt-filled racing adventure.</p> <p>Playing against the clock while trying to steer your way around buoys and avoid the hazing of your opponent could be really tough. If you're skilled, you'll power up and go even faster, but if you fail, you'll get way behind. Thankfully, though, this is a quite attractive N64 game which is even better when racing against a friend.</p> <p>If you're more skilled at racing than me, you'll probably adore Wave Race 64. It's the sort of retro that still holds up today, and it offers a phenomenally fun time, assuming you can react quickly to the choppy waters.</p> <p>Even though I never played any skateboarding games, I really adored Snowboard Kids. I'm not sure what it was about this N64 adventure, but it hooked me hard. Maybe it was the cute, cartoony graphics, or the simple yet well balanced gameplay. But this was one of those games I found myself playing again and again.</p> <p>The simple hook of the game is doing tricks while airborne to get coins, and then using them to buy powerups on tracks. You can have active and support items, such as hurling rocks at your opponents or turning temporarily invisible.</p> <p>Perhaps the most surprising aspect of Snowboard Kids was that it was published by Atlus, who are usually known for JRPGs. But this is just one of those games that is still a ton of fun today, which I can attest to having played it recently with my close friends on their N64.</p> <p>Even though I'm not amazing at racing games, I spent many, many hours playing Mario Kart 64. I barely touched Super Mario Kart, didn't do much with the later entries, but that N64 game was one I kept returning to with friends.</p> <p>There was just something about this game that made it one of the most iconic entries in the longrunning series. Maybe it was the quirky cast of Mario characters, maybe it was tracks like Rainbow Road. But this was a truly fun racing game that appealed to players of various abilities.</p> <p>Mix in various engine classes, a wide class of racers and amazingly fun attack items, and you have a great game in Mario Kart 64. But if that wasn't enough, it also features some of the best courses in the series, complete with really catchy tunes.</p> <p>If you have a need for speed, you need look no further than F-Zero X. This futuristic series featured some of the most blisteringly fast courses and crazy racers you'll see in any series. And honestly, F-Zero X is still probably one of the best racers ever. So even though it's shameful we haven't gotten a sequel, you might not need it either.</p> <p>Death Race offered a truly unhinged way to play the game, and the randomness of X-Cup could keep you playing the game for a long time. So even though it's not the most graphically impressive game, the high framerate and fantasically weird drivers made this a worthy contender.</p> <p>Plasma Powered hovercars turned made racing even more fun, and so long as you can keep up, you'll find one of the best racers ever in F-Zero X. Here's hoping it gets an even better followup on modern consoles one day.</p> <p>If you're looking to get a proper taste of fifth-gen gaming culture, these are the Bucket List Nintendo 64 Games You Need To Play.</p> <p>Share your take with fellow players. Keep it constructive, relevant, and respectful.</p> <p>Log in to share your thoughts, reply to other readers, and keep track of your community activity.</p> <p>No comments yet. Be the first to start the conversation.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://www.dualshockers.com/best-n64-racing-games-that-still-hold-up-today/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5458"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "5 Of The Best Sci-Fi Movie Black Friday Deals Under $20", "image": ["https://www.slashfilm.com/img/gallery/5-of-the-best-sci-fi-movie-black-friday-deals-under-20/l-intro-1795204156.jpg"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T23:30:00+00:00", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T23:30:00+00:00", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5458"}</script> <h2>5 Of The Best Sci-Fi Movie Black Friday Deals Under $20</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://www.slashfilm.com/2323527/best-sci-fi-movies-black-friday-deals-under-20-dollars/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://www.slashfilm.com/img/gallery/5-of-the-best-sci-fi-movie-black-friday-deals-under-20/l-intro-1795204156.jpg" alt="5 Of The Best Sci-Fi Movie Black Friday Deals Under $20" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>We may receive a commission on purchases made from links.</p> <p>Not to paint with too broad of a brush here, but I feel like there is a tremendous amount of crossover between hardcore science fiction fans and physical media collectors. I say this as a proud member of both parties. To that end, Black Friday is a good time for sci-fi lovers who also happen to still buy Blu-rays and 4K Ultra HD copies of movies they love, and 2026 offers no shortage of deals to take advantage of.</p> <p>There are plenty of underrated sci-fi movies people need to watch. There have been plenty of outstanding sci-fi movies released this past year, including "Project Hail Mary." The options are endless and, fortunately, this year's Black Friday offerings cover the spread. We've dug through the sales and picked out a handful of the best options available to prospective buyers out there.</p> <p>From classics in the Criterion Collection to one of the greatest science fiction movies of the 21st century and everything in between, these are five of the best sci-fi movies you can get for under $20 during this year's Black Friday sales. Let's dive in, shall we?</p> <p>Recent entries in the "Godzilla" franchise, most notably "Godzilla Minus One" and "Godzilla Minus Zero," have brought a lot of people to the party. This is a franchise with over 70 years of history though and much, much more to enjoy. For those looking to dig into the character's history a bit more, there's no better way to do that than 1989's "Godzilla vs. Biollante." A little wacky but also breathtaking, it's one of the best "Godzilla" movies of all time.</p> <p>For Black Friday, Amazon has the Criterion Blu-ray on sale for $19.88, which is 50% off. It's tough to get Criterion releases this cheap and this one is truly stellar. Not only is the restoration incredible but it comes with a pretty amazing poster and new artwork. Unfortunately, the 4K is $24.98 and a little above our $20 threshold. That said, the Blu-ray more than cuts the mustard here, in my humble opinion.</p> <p>A grieving scientist blends human DNA with a rose and mutated Godzilla cells to engineer a massive plant-based beast dubbed Biollante. The King of the Monsters then emerges from the sea to fight this new threat. The plot itself is absolutely wild but it features some of the most impressive practical creature effects ever put to film. Biollante, specifically, is a truly masterful work of visual effects. It's nothing shy of incredible and that's precisely why Criterion added it to the prestigious collection.</p> <p>You can grab "Godzilla vs. Biollante" on Blu-ray from Amazon.</p> <p>We arrive at what is likely the best out-and-out bargain on this list. "Mars Attacks!" wasn't a success in its day but for '90s kids of a certain age, it was a formative movie. It was Tim Burton at the apex of his career as a bankable A-list director taking a big, weird swing. It's sci-fi with a capital S and a capital F. It's strange. It's funny. It's violent. It's beyond star-studded. It rocks. And it's a ridiculously great deal right now.</p> <p>As part of Amazon's Black Friday sale, the 4K Ultra HD edition of "Mars Attacks!" is on sale for just $11.99. That's a whopping 60% off. For those who don't want to go through Amazon, it's also on sale via Gruv for $12.99. And Gruv does offer free shipping in a lot of situations, for what it's worth. So for those who don't have Amazon Prime, that may actually be the better option.</p> <p>Earth is invaded by Martians with seemingly unbeatable, superior weaponry and a cruel sense of humor. Delightfully and darkly humorous chaos ensues. This may be the movie that made Burton take a break from Hollywood, but it's also arguably his most underrated work to date. Ahead of its time and wholly unique a fully 30 years later, it's very much worth revisiting. Especially in glorious Ultra HD. Who doesn't want to see Jack Nicholson playing multiple characters at the height of his powers?</p> <p>You can grab "Mars Attacks!" on 4K from Amazon.</p> <p>In the realm of blending sci-fi and horror together, it truly doesn't get much better than 1997's "Event Horizon." Rivaled only by a few stone cold classics such as John Carpenter's "The Thing," this is a true gem of the genre and one that has only gotten better with age. It also recently got an overdue upgrade on 4K Ultra HD, which is included as part of this year's Black Friday offerings.</p> <p>The 4K/Blu-ray combo pack (which also contains a digital code) is on sale on Amazon right now for $16.99. That's "only" 35% off the standard retail price, but it's a damn fine release with some excellent box art that will look great on the ol' physical media shelf. It's not the biggest discount of the bunch but it's a solid price.</p> <p>"Event Horizon" is essentially a haunted house movie in space focused on a state-of-the-art research spacecraft that mysteriously vanished without a trace on its maiden voyage seven years ago. A signal prompts a rescue crew to head out on a bold rescue mission. However, what they uncover is an unimaginable interstellar horror. With a star-studded cast led by Laurence Fishburne and Sam Neill, it's the business.</p> <p>Directed by Paul W.S. Anderson, of "Mortal Kombat" and "Resident Evil" fame, "Event Horizon" is one of the best (and scariest) sci-fi horror movies ever made. It's a worthy addition to any science fiction fan's collection. Even those who don't typically go in for horror.</p> <p>You can grab "Event Horizon" on 4K from Amazon.</p> <p>Certain critics remain very cynical when it comes to the "Transformers" movie franchise. At a certain point, that cynicism was justified as later entries such as "Age of Extinction" and "The Last Knight" overstayed their welcome. But when Michael Bay first brought the robots in disguise to life in live-action in 2007, it was movie magic for people of a certain age. And few movies benefit from a 4K upgrade more than the original "Transformers" live-action movie.</p> <p>Admittedly, this one isn't on a deep discount but the 4K/Blu-ray combo pack with a digital code is just $12.73 on Amazon for Black Friday. That's only a 15% discount from the $14.99 retail price, which is pretty darn good as is. But why pay more for it if you don't have to? Bayhem in Ultra HD for less than $13 screams bargain to me.</p> <p>The first movie in the series sees the heroic Autobots and the evil Decepticons come to Earth, with a clue to the ultimate power held by a teenager, Sam Witwicky (Shia LaBeouf). Come for the nostalgia, stay for some of the most impressive CGI you're ever likely to see in a mainstream blockbuster.</p> <p>When I participated in /Film's 50 hottest movie takes list a few years ago, I argued that "Transformers" was one of Bay's very best movies. A lot of people who came of age in the '90s would probably agree with me, no matter how one feels about the later entries in the series. With Bay and Steven Spielberg returning for the next "Transformers" movie, now feels like the perfect time to revisit the beginning of this multi-billion-dollar, globally popular franchise.</p> <p>You can grab "Transformers" on 4K from Amazon.</p> <p>Last but certainly not least, we come to one of the best science fiction movies of recent memory. Director George Miller's "Mad Max: Fury Road" wasn't just the best reviewed movie of 2015, it's earned its place as one of the best sci-fi movies ever made, as well as one of the best action movies ever made. Dare I say, it's just one of the best movies period? The point is, nobody is going to argue against owning this movie and, fortunately, it's on a deep discount during Black Friday.</p> <p>The 4K Ultra HD edition of Miller's modern masterpiece is going for $14.99 right now — a steep 57% off the usual retail price of $34.98. That makes it not only probably the best movie on this list, but one of the best deals as well. It's hard to go wrong. Even for those who may own this one on Blu-ray or DVD, it's well worth the upgrade. Few movies will benefit more from the extra fidelity on offer from UHD.</p> <p>The movie sees the tyrannical Immortan Joe enslave survivors inside his desert fortress, the Citadel. When Furiosa (Charlize Theron) leads the despot's five wives in a daring escape, she forges an alliance with the quiet loner Max (Tom Hardy) in a fortified armored War Rig. The most epic car chase in cinema history ensues.</p> <p>I can't say anything about "Fury Road" that hasn't been said by other people more eloquently. I don't need to convince anyone that this movie kicks wholesale a**. But I can humbly suggest that if you have $15 to spare, this is one of the best ways to spend it during Black Friday.</p> <p>You can grab "Mad Max: Fury Road" on 4K from Amazon.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://www.slashfilm.com/2323527/best-sci-fi-movies-black-friday-deals-under-20-dollars/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; Here’s how it works.</p> <p>Blake Shelton has never shied away from sharing lessons he’s learned from being the stepdad of Gwen Stefani’s kids. In fact, he’s been candid about realizing that he can be someone they can lean on. Now, he’s revealed the “most cliché” about him, and it’s both very sweet and shows how he and his wife's kids lean on and support each other.</p> <p>Stefani shares three kids with her ex, Gavin Rossdale. Kingston is 20, Zuma is 18 and Apollo is 12. Shelton specifically opened up about spending quality time with Zuma and Apollo during an interview with Taste of Country, explaining that his after-school routine with them is probably the “most cliché dad thing” he does:</p> <p>I guess the most cliché dad thing I do every single day when the kids get home from school, Apollo, our youngest, we throw the football for about 30 minutes. And he’s got this thing, he loves to jump on the trampoline while I throw the football to him so he can do the crazy Odell Beckham catches, DeAndre Hopkins stuff.</p> <p>When he was asked if he also gets on the trampoline, Shelton joked that “just throwing the ball wears [him] out.” There’s that signature humor we saw on The Voice coming out. However, overall, the singer was very clear that he loves doing this with his kid. And I really can’t think of anything more father-son coded than playing catch in the yard together. So, yes, while cliché, it’s incredibly sweet.</p> <p>Adding to that, he revealed how he spends time with Zuma after school too. I have to say, this sounds right up Blake Shelton’s alley, as he said:</p> <p>Zuma, we’ll spend about 20 minutes with him and his guitar. And it’s not me showing him stuff, it’s him showing me stuff that, you know, ‘Have you heard this song? Have you heard of this artist? Listen to this song.’ It’s almost every single day. I love it; I can’t wait until they get home.</p> <p>Shelton has always been very supportive of his stepson’s music career, and I love that they bond over it on a daily basis. I can’t help but wonder if they have a shared playlist and if the “God Gave Me You” singer’s love for country music has rubbed off on him.</p> <p>I’d assume Shelton has fallen into a really good routine with these kids, considering he and Stefani have been together for a long time now. The former The Voice coaches met in 2014 on the show, and they began dating in 2015. The singers then announced their engagement in 2020, and they tied the knot in 2021. So, the “Boys ‘Round Here” singer has been in these kids’ lives for well over a decade (which is also most of Apollo’s life).</p> <p>Overall, these comments from Blake Shelton go to show how strong he and Gwen Stefani are, and how tight he is with her kids. Now, I hope these daily activities continue in the Shelton-Stefani household, because yes, they’re cliché, but they’re cliché in the absolute best way.</p> <p>Riley Utley is the Weekend Editor at CinemaBlend.
