Never miss a kick-off!</p> <p>Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.</p> <p>A case of mistaken identity sparked one of the most bizarre Premier League incidents ever</p> <p>When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.</p> <p>Controversial refereeing decisions have been a part of football for a long as the game has been around, but one of the most bizarre incidents in recent memory occurred during Arsenal’s infamous 6-0 defeat to Chelsea in March 2014.</p> <p>After spotting an Arsenal handball on the goal line, referee Andrer Marriner reached for his pocket and flashed a red card to Kieran Gibbs.</p> <p>However, the issue here was that it was actually Alex Oxlade-Chamberlain who had handled the ball, and in this pre-VAR era, Gibbs was forced into an early bath, despite the protests of players from both sides.</p> <p>As Gibbs looks back on the incident more than a decade on, he remembers the confusion, the frustration and the surreal nature of the incident which wouldn’t be allowed to happen these days.</p> <p>“It feels like a blur now, and it did at the time too,” he reflects to FourFourTwo. “It’s heated in the moment and you’re trying to focus on the game.”</p> <p>The incident came during a calamitous first half for the Gunners, who found themselves 2-0 down after just seven minutes in what was Arsene Wenger’s 1,000th game in charge of the club.</p> <p>The handball incident came in the 17th minute, when Oxlade-Chamberlain threw his left hand at an Eden Hazard shot, tipping it around the post. Marriner pointed to the spot and the referee was not for turning, despite widespread protests from both sets of players.</p> <p>The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.</p> <p>The Belgian would slot home the penalty to effectively end the game as a contest, with Gibbs still finding it remarkable that such an incident would occur, given the technology now used in the game.</p> <p>“It feels crazy that there was no VAR, because you don’t have those kinds of issues anymore,” he continues.</p> <p>“It was a surreal and very frustrating moment that was unique in the Premier League, so it brought a lot of attention.</p> <p>“Both captains and the referee were telling me I was definitely going off.</p> <p>“After a few minutes of protesting, I needed to listen to them.</p> <p>“It was a big error from the referee, who apologised later and the red card was rescinded.”</p> <p>For more than a decade, Joe Mewis has worked in football journalism as a reporter and editor. margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://cdn.mos.cms.futurecdn.net/x245qNaNNPbCoP2C9JnUNP-2560-80.jpg" alt="I tested the Xreal Aura glasses for 2 weeks — amazing displays and immersive spatial computing let down by a lack of apps" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Keep exploring and earning more as a member.</p> <p>Unlock and manage exclusive Tom’s Guide member rewards.</p> <p>Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.</p> <p>When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.</p> <p>Xreal and Google's first foray into XR glasses is a fascinating look at the future of AR and VR devices. But that also means you're prototyping something that will get better, but maybe not yet.</p> <p>Why you can trust Tom's Guide Our writers and editors spend hours analyzing and reviewing products, services, and apps to help find what's best for you. Find out more about how we test, analyze, and rate.</p> <p>For quite awhile I have firmly believed that smart glasses are the next device that will evolve the future of computing, much like the iPhone heralded the next 20 years of tech progression. And this is despite the current "pervert glasses" backlash.</p> <p>I am fascinated by the sudden influx of AR and XR glasses that offer immersive environments and while also looking "normal." I've already tried Meta VR Glasses and Snap Specs, but neither are actually available for everyone yet. The Google, Qualcomm and Xreal collaboration Aura glasses are.</p> <p>All three glasses could be considered VR-lite, but I think they're about finding a use between AI glasses and wearing a full headset.</p> <p>In that way, the Auras are the escaped experiment worth investigating. Following the Samsung Galaxy XR headset, these are just the second device to run Google's Android XR platform natively.</p> <p>This first stab at an XR headset that I think most people will want is impressive and a great example of what Android XR could be, but it also means you're the guinea pig with all that implies.</p> <p>Xreal S1 andQualcomm Snapdragon Reality Elite Platform</p> <p>Right when the Aura launched on November 17, I put together some early impressions of the glassese. For the most part, my thoughts haven't changed. But after using the glasses more, some plusses and minuses have solidified.</p> <p>I am not an advocate for VR as it currently works with bulky, isolating headsets. However, I can still be impressed virtual reality experiences.</p> <p>Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.</p> <p>The Xreal Aura provides that with 6DoF spatial computing. The two temple cameras and the nose bridge camera enable pinch gesture controls and you can always use the puck as trackpad, which I defaulted to more often that not.</p> <p>The pinch controls work well. Occasionally the hitbox was a hair off but I didn't notice lag when I adjusted. I will note that in darker environments, it didn't always work, which isn't totally surprising but something to know.</p> <p>Out of the box, the Auras come with a variety of mixed-reality experiences, from a VR tour of the Galapagos, to some games and a 360 version of Google Maps that is a delight to explore.</p> <p>VR enthusiasts will find the 70-degree field of view limiting but I wasn't bothered by it. Watching movies feels like you're in a theater for yourself, and extending my desktop workspace was seamless via the wired accessory cable (or wireless for some devices).</p> <p>These might be the best displays Xreal has made to date. I tested the refreshed Xreal One Pro Plus a couple of weeks ago and thought it was an improvement, and now the Aura is taking it even further.</p> <p>Xreal lists the resolution of the Aura's as 1,920 x 1,200 per eye (1200p). It makes for sharp images, especially in games and media. It feels premium.</p> <p>The color gamut isn't robust and the Aura doesn't offer HDR like the One Pro Plus, but if you don't need that level of display detail, these look good. Plus, with 1200 nits of brightness, the display can handle a number of settings. And the electrochromic dimming gets pretty dark, meaning you should feel more immersed.</p> <p>Personally, I have never had great luck reading text as is on AR glasses, and while the Aura's are better, you may find text blurry. Fortunately, you can zoom in, which helps.</p> <p>One advantage the Xreal Aura glasses have over Meta's VR Glasses and the Snap Specs is the built-in Google ecosystem. Snap and Meta have both promised that they're building up their developer connections to create more apps for their glasses, but Google's slew of in-house designs and third-party apps is already robust.</p> <p>On the productivity side, you've got the entire Google suite from Docs and Drive to your calendar and Gmail. Plus, there's the Google Play Store with normal phone games and a small but growing number of XR-specific apps.</p> <p>Moving forward, as Android XR grows, this will be what sets the Aura and future Android XR devices apart from Snap and Meta.</p> <p>Here's the rub: you don't really need XR glasses yet. Xreal's own lineup of AR glasses are already great for productivity, gaming and watching content by connecting to your laptop, mini PC, phone or gaming device.</p> <p>Because of that, the flaws do stand out a bit more.</p> <p>If we're thinking of the Aura as VR-lite, especially compared to the Meta VR Glasses, then the biggest missing component for the Xreal Aura glasses is games.</p> <p>Yes, I did try Demeo, which was fun, and HoloChess, which felt like the Star Wars version of chess. But from what I could tell, there aren't many native XR/VR games on the Play Store, while the Meta Quest Store has hundreds, and Meta promised at least 75 launch titles specifically designed for the VR glasses next year.</p> <p>You can play 2D games on the Play Store, which I did with some games I already have on my account. Xreal is selling its own first-party controller made with GameSir, but you can use any Bluetooth controller. Again, you can connect other Xreal glasses to your phone, laptop, or gaming device with a controller as well.</p> <p>It's not clear how long it'll take native Android XR experiences to catch up to Meta, but for now, Meta has the lead.</p> <p>Xreal rates the battery life of the Aura puck between three and four hours. I found that to be pretty accurate. In my testing watching an hour-long YouTube video and then playing some games for about 20 minutes knocked the battery life from 100% to about 80%.</p> <p>I debated putting this as a flaw because I'm not sure if you want to keep these glasses on for more than three hours. Perhaps that is the sweet spot. Still, for anything longer, you'll need to recharge.</p> <p>That said, heat becomes a concern. The puck can get warm, but it does include a fan to dissipate heat. Surprisingly, the glasses can get quite warm, especially in the area that runs across your forehead.</p> <p>General use seemed okay, but at one point I accidentally turned on screen recording and I could feel the glasses heating up. It never got laptop-panini levels of hot, but as the tech ages, I foresee this being a problem.</p> <p>The Xreal Auras have just three cameras. Two on the temples and one on the nose bridge. As mentioned, I find the hand tracking pretty decent and pinch gestures work well.</p> <p>However, I've also worn the Meta VR Glasses, which have 6 external cameras and four internal ones. Fundamentally, Xreal adding seven more cameras would change the design of the glasses, which you can see in the ski-goggle look of Meta's forthcoming platform.</p> <p>Still, eye tracking makes navigating the XR space better. And compared to Meta, where you can leave your hand on the table or in your lap and still use gesture controls, the three-camera Aura setup is more limiting.</p> <p>The pushback is the puck trackpad, which I used most frequently. I am comfortable with trackpads and mice, and the puck is a nice midway point.</p> <p>The puck is a mini-PC with glasses attached, but that doesn't make navigation the best without accessories or the trackpad.</p> <p>If Xreal's next iteration can add eye tracking or even a couple more external cameras, the already good hand tracking would be even better.</p> <p>I find the Xreal Aura glasses fascinating, and when its rivals finally launch, I think a new space in the tech world will open up that is more than just pervert glasses and bulky headsets. Perhaps I've given these glasses more grace than future versions will deserve, but for a first go in this space, I'm excited by the potential.</p> <p>For many, I'm not sure the $1,000+ price tag is cheap enough to dive in, but for the curious and the early adopters, this is a device of potential. It hints at what mixed reality wearables could be.</p> <p>When I use the Aura, I get what it's supposed to be. It's the taste of a Vision Pro or Quest headset in a lighter, more comfortable package. Xreal has set the bar; it'll be up to everyone to see if they can raise it.</p> <p>Follow Tom's Guide on Google News and add us as a preferred source to get our up-to-date news, analysis, and reviews in your feeds.</p> <p>Scott Younker is the West Coast Reporter at Tom’s Guide. He covers all the lastest tech news. He’s been involved in tech since 2011 at various outlets and is on an ongoing hunt to build the easiest to use home media system. height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Keep exploring and earning more as a member.</p> <p>Read more news stories for free, receive our daily newsletter, post comments, play games and earn badges.</p> <p>Unlimited access to our exclusive features, expert analysis and in-depth interviews, all ad-free.</p> <p>Rigorously fact-checked science, expert commentary and an ad-free experience built for those who want to go beyond the headlines</p> <p>Not ready for Pro? Join Live Science Plus</p> <p>Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.</p> <p>Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.</p> <p>Sign up for the latest discoveries, groundbreaking research and fascinating breakthroughs that impact you and the wider world direct to your inbox.</p> <p>Feed your curiosity with an exclusive mystery every week, delivered straight to your inbox before it's seen anywhere else.</p> <p>Sign up to our free science & technology newsletter for your weekly fix of fascinating articles, quick quizzes, amazing images, and more</p> <p>Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!</p> <p>Sign up to our monthly entertainment newsletter to keep up with all our coverage of the latest sci-fi and space movies, tv shows, games and books.</p> <p>Discover this week's must-see night sky events, moon phases, and stunning astrophotos. Sign up for our skywatching newsletter and explore the universe with us!</p> <p>Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.</p> <p>A new positioning technology could make autonomous vehicles and robotaxis more reliable at recharging themselves.</p> <p>When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.</p> <p>Researchers in Japan have developed a new technology that could enable electric vehicles (EVs) to charge "simply by parking".</p> <p>Wireless charging is still relatively nascent in the EV market, but scientists hope that integrating more wireless charging devices into roads and parking spaces will move the EV industry away from bulky plug-in infrastructure. It uses the same wireless power transfer technology found in modern smartphones and consumer electronics: a transmitter coil inside in a ground pad generates an oscillating magnetic field, which induces an electric current in a receiver coil underneath the vehicle. This current recharges the EV's battery cells.</p> <p>However, small misalignments between the two coils can affect how quickly and efficiently the battery recharges. This new system uses short-range radio signals to determine the position of the vehicle's magnetic charging coil relative to the charging device in the ground, including its distance and angle. This helps the EV and its driver get into the optimal position for efficient charging.</p> <p>The technology could be particularly useful for driverless "robotaxis" and other autonomous vehicles that need to be able to recharge quickly and efficiently without human assistance, the researchers said. They detailed their findings in a study published Aug. 26 in the IEEE Open Journal of Vehicular Technology.</p> <p>"We hope this research will lead to a future where EVs charge simply by parking, much like smartphones placed on wireless chargers," Ryotaro Hada, first author of the study and a master's student at the University of Osaka's Graduate School of Information Science and Technology, said in a statement.</p> <p>Existing methods for aligning an EV's charging coil with a wireless charging pad use magnetic sensors or cameras. Magnetic systems work best when the coil is close to the pad, while cameras can struggle in rain, poor lighting or when dirt obscures the pad, the researchers explained.</p> <p>This new system uses four ultra-wideband radio devices mounted around the vehicle's charging coil to communicate with four antennas around the charging pad.</p> <p>Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.</p> <p>By measuring how long radio waves take to travel between the ultra-wideband devices and the antennas, as well as detecting tiny differences in how the signals arrive at each of the vehicle's sensors, the system can pinpoint the position and angle of the EV's charging coil relative to the ground pad.</p> <p>The same technology is used in some digital car keys. They use short radio pulses to detect when the driver is close by, allowing the driver to unlock their vehicle without requiring a physical key.</p> <p>In laboratory experiments using a scaled-down vehicle model, the system worked out where the vehicle's charging coil was to within 0.09 inches (2.32 millimeters) of its actual position and which direction it was facing to within 0.25 degrees. Correcting for distortions in the radio signals reduced the positioning error by around fivefold, the team said.</p> <p>The researchers noted that the system still needs to be tested outdoors, on full-size EVs and under real charging conditions.</p> <p>Owen Hughes is a freelance writer and editor specializing in data and digital technologies. Previously a senior editor at ZDNET, Owen has been writing about tech for more than a decade, during which time he has covered everything from AI, cybersecurity and supercomputers to programming languages and public sector IT. Owen is particularly interested in the intersection of technology, life and work ­– in his previous roles at ZDNET and TechRepublic, he wrote extensively about business leadership, digital transformation and the evolving dynamics of remote work.</p> <p>You must confirm your public display name before commenting</p> <p>Please logout and then login again, you will then be prompted to enter your display name.</p> <p>Live Science is part of Future US Inc, an international media group and leading digital publisher. </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>In October, Amazon launched a brand-new round of Kindle e-readers, making it even harder to pick one. It doesn't help that the new 2026 Kindles are more expensive, too. I've spent years testing Kindles for Mashable, and I can tell you that the latest batch is impressive, and it shows exactly why Kindles are still the most popular e-readers.</p> <p>After testing the newest Kindles, we have a new favorite. The Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition has edged out the standard Paperwhite as the best Kindle to buy in 2026. However, that doesn't mean it's the best Kindle for everyone, and so we've tested the whole Kindle lineup to make expert recommendations for every type of reader.</p> <p>These are the tech, tools, and products — from laptops to e-readers, from earbuds to robovacs, and more — that Mashable ranks best in class.</p> <p>Once you have a Kindle, there's still so much you can do to make the most of it. For avid readers, Stuff Your Kindle Day and Libby let you cross every last book off your annual reading list. You can even buy Kindle accessories like remote page-turners and gooseneck stands so you don't have to hold your Kindle while reading. (By the way, we love Amazon's new Kindle Click and Page Turn Cover, and the latter even reminds us of the cult-favorite Kindle Oasis, RIP.)</p> <p>So, after extensive testing, reading so many books, checking in with BookTok authors, and attending BookCon, I created this guide to the best Kindles of 2026. Plus, with Black Friday coming up, I've found the best deals on Kindles.</p> <p>Yes, Kindles are on sale for the Amazon Black Friday sale, so if you're buying an e-reader as a Christmas gift, this is your chance to save. Amazon kicked off its Black Friday sale on Nov. 19, and with it, all of the 2024 Kindle models dropped in price. The best deal is on the 2024 Kindle Paperwhite, one of our favorites. The 2024 e-reader saw a $50 price increase recently, but now it's down to $129.99, which is even cheaper than before. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile</p> <p>The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile</p> <p>But there are plenty of other Kindle models on sale, including the 2024 Kindle Colorsoft (now $120 off) and the Kindle Scribe (now $150 off). However, it's only 2024 models on sale so far, as Amazon hasn't marked down its new 2026 models during Black Friday.</p> <p>Fortunately, we still love the 2024 Kindles, and they even work with the new Kindle Click.</p> <p>On Oct. 1, Amazon announced a redesign of the entire Kindle collection. The new Kindles have vivid new colors like ube, and for the first time, you can buy these e-readers with aluminum casings. The new Kindles are also thinner and lighter than before. They also have a removable back panel so you can eventually replace the battery, though we have had some problems with this feature.</p> <p>Kindle Paperwhite (16GB) — $199.99 to $219.99</p> <p>Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition (32GB) — $249.99</p> <p>Kindle Colorsoft Signature Edition (32GB) — $319.99</p> <p>Read our full review of the Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition (2026).</p> <p>Most readers: Amazon's latest release, the Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition, is the best Kindle. While we previously saw it as an upgrade pick, the new aluminum casing makes it essential with a sleeker, sturdier case. Inside, it's still the same speedy Paperwhite we love. It turns pages without stuttering, never slowing down when you’re in the thick of the action.</p> <p>People who read on the go: The 7-inch screen has adjustable brightness and warmth but it also auto-adjusts. That means if you're commuting or the sun changes overhead, the Paperwhite Signature Edition will adjust the screen based on your environment. The 2026 model is lighter and thinner than ever and fits comfortably in small crossbody bags. Plus, it’s waterproof, keeping it safe from poolside vacations and spilled water bottles in bags.</p> <p>When picking the best Kindle, it always comes down to this question — Paperwhite or Paperwhite Signature Edition? Historically, I've preferred the Paperwhite, finding that the Signature Edition doesn't have enough features to justify its price. The 2026 Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition finally fixes that. With a new aluminum case, the Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition feels like a high-end piece of tech. With a thinner body and square edges, this new Kindle feels more like a smartphone or tablet than it does an e-reader.</p> <p>But inside the more advanced chassis is still the same e-reader you love. It's fast with a high contrast 300ppi resolution. The matte display is glare-free and easy on the eyes. With adjustable warmth and brightness you can read with it in bright summer sun at the beach and then turn it down and warm it up when you're in a dim bedroom. And speaking of the beach, its IPX8 rating makes it safe to use in the bath or at the pool.</p> <p>It is a more expensive e-reader at $249.99, especially compared to the Paperwhite's $199.99, but here's what you get for an extra $50. The upgraded aluminum case makes that $50 nearly worth it compared to the plastic exterior of the Paperwhite, but the Signature Edition also has an ad-free lockscreen, 32GB of storage compared to the Paperwhite's 16GB, wireless charging, auto-adjusting brightness, and best of all, compatibility with the new Kindle Page Turn Cover.</p> <p>Plus, the Kindle Paperwhite Signature comes in more exciting color options. While the Paperwhite just comes in graphite, the Signature Edition also comes in a stunning seaglass green.</p> <p>Read our full review of the Kindle (2026).</p> <p>Budget shoppers: Especially in light of the Kindle price increases, if you want to read books on an e-reader but need a budget-friendly model, the regular Kindle is the best option. It recently got an update, too, and the 2026 Kindle is better than ever. It’s the smallest Kindle, with a 6-inch screen that makes it pocket-sized (if you have big enough pockets). But with a budget device, keep in mind you have to make some sacrifices. The basic Kindle isn't waterproof nor does it have adjustable warm light.</p> <p>Amazon made some updates to the 2026 Kindle that make it worthy of its higher price tag. It has a new, flush front design for a sleeker look. Plus, the design team made a change we've been dying for — they moved the power button to the side of the device. Plenty of specs have stayed the same between generations. It still has a 6-inch screen, 300 ppi resolution, and six weeks of battery life.</p> <p>What truly makes it stand out is just how lightweight it is. The 2026 Kindle weighs 18 grams (.66 ounces) less than the previous generation and it's a noticeable difference. It feels like you're barely holding anything; plus, it's even better for travel as it takes up very little space and weighs just half a pound.</p> <p>It's a speedy little e-reader, too. Its screen is responsive and loads books within seconds. The only thing that could make the e-reader better is if Amazon added an adjustable warm light and made it waterproof. However, according to Amazon, the lack of those features keeps it affordable.</p> <p>The device is now available in its basic format in either ube or graphite. However, it's also available with an aluminum backing, making it more like a tablet. The aluminum Kindle is more expensive at $189.99, but it does come with 32GB of storage.</p> <p>Read our full review of the Kindle Paperwhite (2024).</p> <p>If you don't like the new prices for Amazon's new 2026 Kindles, then you can still buy the 2024 Kindle Paperwhite. Up until October, this was our pick as the best Kindle, and it's just as good now. Best of all, during big sales like Black Friday, you can pick up this popular e-reader for $129.99. The new model costs $199.99, which is a big ask for most readers.</p> <p>The screen's front light is vibrant and has adjustable brightness, meaning it can adjust when transitioning from reading in the sun or shade. It also comes with adjustable screen warmth, perfect for evening readers who want to give their eyes a chance to relax. It’s lightweight and fits comfortably in small crossbody bags. Plus, it’s waterproof, keeping it safe against poolside vacations and spilled water bottles in bags.</p> <p>I used this Kindle for two years, and it's as good as the day I took it out of the box. Plus, it doesn't suffer from the back cover design flaw readers have said they've encountered with the 2026 Kindle e-readers.</p> <p>There's a recurring question we see about Kindles: What’s the difference between the Kindle Paperwhite and Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition? The Signature Edition has twice the storage of the Paperwhite (32GB), auto-adjusting screen brightness, an ad-free lockscreen, and wireless charging, and it costs $40 more. Sure, those things are helpful, but I don’t think those extra features are necessary.</p> <p>Not a ton changed between the 2022 and 2024 models of the Kindle Paperwhite, but we can be certain that the 2024 Kindle Paperwhite is the sleeker, faster e-reader. The 2024 Paperwhite got a slight upgrade in screen quality compared to the 2022 Paperwhite. While the resolution is still 300 ppi, it appears to have higher contrast, straying away from the like-paper look and edging closer to the look of a tablet. Don’t worry, though — unlike tablets, the Kindle Paperwhite still has a gloriously glare-free screen. It still has an IPX68 waterproof rating, so you can get some leisurely reading in the bath.</p> <p>Note: On Oct. 1, 2026, Amazon released a new version of the Kindle Paperwhite Kids. We are currently in the process of testing the new e-reader.</p> <p>Young readers: If you're raising a voracious reader, the Kindle Paperwhite Kids is the best Kindle for kids. It's waterproof, which means it stands up to juice spills and dips in the pool. Plus, it comes with a Kindle case included. It's designed to be kid-proof, not just physically, but also with built-in parental controls. If you're concerned about monitoring your kid's bookshelf, this Kindle allows you to approve downloads by kids and displays age-friendly titles that kids can easily access.</p> <p>More than anything else, a Kindle for kids has to be waterproof, which is why the Kindle Paperwhite Kids is the best choice. It comes with everything we like about the standard Paperwhite: a beautiful, high-resolution screen that works faster than any previous generation.</p> <p>But what makes it great for kids is that it's a distraction-free device. It doesn't come with ads, notifications, or the temptation of games or video. I also like that it's a bit heavier than the standard Paperwhite, giving it a sturdier design. Plus, the built-in case adds an extra level of protection.</p> <p>The parental controls are easy to use and set up, and the device also comes with a six-month free subscription to Amazon Kids+, which provides unlimited access to thousands of age-appropriate books. The library of books is easy for kids to navigate and offers classics like Lois Lowry's The Giver and modern classics like Diary of a Wimpy Kid.</p> <p>What I don't love is the price, especially now that it just rose by $50. At $229.99, it's more expensive than the standard Paperwhite. If you want to go for a budget kids' pick, I'd go for a standard Kindle, which you can set up as a kids' device during setup to ensure parental controls and add an Amazon Kids+ subscription for $5.99 per month. Plus, as your kid ages, you won't have to upgrade to a more teen-friendly e-reader.</p> <p>For color enthusiasts: The Kindle Colorsoft would probably be a better option for kids than for adults, serving as a bridge between colorful storybooks and more challenging chapter books. But it works for adults too, especially those who want color or to use the Page Turn Cover.</p> <p>The Kindle Colorsoft makes some modest improvements over its predecessor, introducing the same design changes as the rest of Amazon's 2026 Kindle lineup. That means the new Kindle Colorsoft has a slimmer build, magnetic Pogo pins, and edge-to-edge color, so the bezels now match the device's color. Overall, it's an improvement, but it doesn't solve the problems of the original device, nor fully justify the $289.99 price (or $319.99 for the Signature Edition).</p> <p>Unfortunately, E Ink technology has extremely dim and muted colors. Even if you crank the vibrancy settings all the way to the max, the colors are disappointing. When you compare the Kindle Colorsoft side-by-side with graphic novels or books with illustrations, the differences are extremely obvious. If you want to read comics or similar color artwork digitally, you're better off with a tablet.</p> <p>With those major caveats, if you want a Kindle with color, this is the best option. On the plus side, we love the fig colorway (it matches the burgundy iPhone), and the color lockscreen artwork does look great.</p> <p>We have yet to thoroughly vet the new Kindle Paperwhite, but before the device's release, we got a sneak peek of the new models. We were impressed by the new aluminum models of the Kindle Paperwhite. Plus, I'm pleased to see that the power button has been moved to the side of the device. Based on how much we've loved previous generations of the Kindle Paperwhite, we feel comfortable saying the new gen will be a good purchase.</p> <p>Read our full review of the Kindle Scribe (3rd Generation).</p> <p>Note takers and annotators: If, looking through your bookshelf, your books have dog-eared pages, highlighted lines, and scribbles in the margins, your instinct might be that an e-reader isn't for you, but I'd like to introduce you to the Kindle Scribe. It's the only Kindle with writing capabilities and comes with an included stylus pen that magnetically attaches to the side of the e-reader; it's ready for all your handwritten thoughts.</p> <p>The pen moves nicely across the screen, mimicking the texture of writing on paper. Its larger screen also makes it better for writing and reading PDFs.</p> <p>In addition to a library of books, the Kindle Scribe also comes with a journal section where you can take notes. The notes feature is especially great for students who want to handwrite notes, convert their handwriting into text, and manage documents through Google Drive.</p> <p>The 2025 Kindle Scribe got a much-needed upgrade. All the good stuff has held over with the addition of some major improvements.</p> <p>It's still big, with an 11-inch display, which does make it a little more awkward to hold while reading, but for writing or annotating, it's optimal. Unlike other tablets (I'm looking at you, iPads) that require you to buy a pen separately, the Kindle Scribe comes with a pen that conveniently attaches to the side so it doesn't get lost. However, it's not the most secure magnetic hold, so be careful not to bump the pen off accidentally.</p> <p>Writing on the Kindle Scribe is a joy. Before testing this e-reader, I used my iPad and Apple Pencil to annotate readings, but I never loved it because the screen felt too slick for the Apple Pencil. The Kindle Scribe does not suffer this problem, as the matte screen lends some friction to the pen, making it feel like you're writing on paper. I enjoyed writing with the pen, and I swear my handwriting looked better on the Kindle Scribe than on my iPad.</p> <p>It finally got a processing update that makes it as speedy as every other Kindle. Gone is the lag when switching between journals and books. Plus, the displays have greatly improved, too, as the 2025 Kindle Scribe has virtually no ghosting. Like the Kindle Paperwhite, the Scribe's battery life lasts 12 weeks. It also has multiple storage options, as you can choose between 32GB or 64GB.</p> <p>It's not your only option if you want a Kindle that can write. The Scribe Colorsoft is also available, and while we appreciate that model, it's quite a bit more expensive. The 2025 Scribe is a better value, it just doesn't have the wow factor of color.</p> <p>For years, Kindle prices have stayed stable, but in August 2026, Amazon finally increased prices. Every model except the Kindle Scribe received a price hike. We can thank RAMageddon, as an Amazon spokesperson told Mashable that the increases were necessary due to rising memory and component costs.</p> <p>Our advice? If you're on a budget, the 2024 Kindles are still very good e-readers.</p> <p>If you're concerned about buying the newest tech, that's not an issue since, as of October 2026, there are nine new models to shop.</p> <p>Before you choose a Kindle, you need to ask one question first: Do you want it to be waterproof? If you prefer reading near a body of water — whether in the bath, by the pool, or at the beach — that immediately narrows down your options. Since the Kindle Oasis is no longer available, your waterproof options are the Kindle Paperwhite, Paperwhite Signature Edition, Colorsoft Signature Edition, and Paperwhite Kids.</p> <p>Beyond water resistance, here are a few other features to consider:</p> <p>Display size: The basic Kindle has a 6-inch display, while the largest has a 10.2-inch display.</p> <p>Storage capacity: Kindles range from 16GB to 32GB of storage capacity.</p> <p>Battery life: The new Kindles generally have outstanding batteries. On the low end, they last up to six weeks per charge, while others last up to 12 weeks.</p> <p>Writing capability: The Kindle Scribe is the only model that allows you to write with the included stylus.</p> <p>The latest Kindles all boast a maximum brightness of 94 nits and a 300 ppi, high-resolution display with zero glare, so you can enjoy sharp text and images at any viewing angle. Kindles are also equipped with WiFi connectivity, allowing you to download all the reading material you could ever want. Plus, you can listen to audiobooks on Audible via the built-in Bluetooth connection.</p> <p>And if you were a die-hard Kindle Oasis fan and are attached to page-turning buttons, your best option is to get the Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition with the Page Turn Cover.</p> <p>Out of all the e-readers I've tested (and I've tested a lot), Kindles have the best compatibility with Libby. I love the digital platform because I can read for free and support my local library. Some other e-readers require you to download your library e-book to a computer and then upload it to an e-reader, but with Kindles, your e-books are sent straight to your device with a click.</p> <p>Libby allows you to read free e-books on a Kindle. Through Libby, you can borrow e-books through libraries, and all you need is a library card. Inside the Libby app, select the "Read with Kindle" option when you borrow a book, which allows you to send these e-books to your Kindle library.</p> <p>Another option for finding free e-books is through Stuff Your Kindle events. These are author-driven events that offer limited-time deals on e-books, often making them heavily discounted or totally free.</p> <p>Through my testing and daily use, I've spent a lot of time with Kindles, learning how to make the most of them. I've written extensively on all the ways to maximize your Kindle, so be sure to browse through my guides and explainers:</p> <p>How to make the most of your Kindle: 8 features you need to use</p> <p>The best Kindle accessories for reading in luxury</p> <p>What is Stuff Your Kindle Day? The best way to fill your e-reader with free books</p> <p>I've read hundreds of free Kindle books with Libby. Here's how.</p> <p>What is Kindle Rewards? Earn free credits when shopping the Kindle store</p> <p>What is Kindle+? A library of books at your fingertips for just $12 a month.</p> <p>Where to get free e-books: Load up your e-reader without spending a dime</p> <p>Prime members can get a free e-book every month with Amazon First Reads</p> <p>How to download EPUB files on your Kindle</p> <p>The Mashable team is split on this question. Our resident Kindle expert Samantha Mangino (yours truly) recommends most people opt for the ad-supported version. However, Mashable Tech Editor Timothy Beck Werth recommends paying a little more to get a Kindle without lockscreen ads. Though this increases the price by $20 to $25, you won't be subjected to annoying ads for books every time you lock your e-reader. Instead, you'll see black-and-white photographs of writing implements and other book motifs.</p> <p>When you're buying a Kindle, you'll notice that Amazon selects the "Lockscreen Ad Supported" version by default. Shoppers must manually select "Without Lockscreen Ads" if they want to remove advertisements from the lock screen. This increases the cost of the base Kindle from $149.99 to $169.99; the Kindle Paperwhite increases from $199.99 to $219.99. For serious readers who hate ads, we think it's worth the added cost.</p> <p>In the past, the Paperwhite has been Amazon's flagship e-reader, with the Paperwhite Signature Edition being the more luxe option. However, with the 2026 redesign, it seems like the script has been flipped, with Amazon pushing the Signature Edition while the standard Paperwhite takes a back seat.</p> <p>Plenty remains the same between the devices. They have the same 7-inch screen with 300 ppi resolution and adjustable warmth and brightness. Both are waterproof with IPX8 ratings and 12-week battery life. The new devices also have easy-to-open back panels to make replacing the battery that much easier.</p> <p>The Paperwhite is the cheaper option at $199.99, but it comes with only 16GB of storage (which, to be fair, is still plenty) and is available in graphite with a plastic casing. The Signature Edition is more expensive at $249.99, but comes with 32GB of storage and is available in the aluminum finish in seaglass green and graphite. Beyond its nicer finish, it also has an ad-free lockscreen, automatic front-light adjustment, a rotating screen, and wireless charging. The real perk of the Paperwhite Signature Edition is its compatibility with the Page Turn Cover, which adds page-turning buttons to your Kindle.</p> <p>As of May 20, 2026, Amazon no longer provides software updates or web services to devices released in 2012 or earlier, essentially bricking them. Users will still be able to read currently downloaded books on the device, but to add new books, they will need to connect the device to a computer. This is a huge hit, especially to Libby users who need web service access to read borrowed books.</p> <p>Owners of these Kindle models are obviously upset, as it means they'll need to purchase a new device to maintain access to all features. While some are considering switching to a different e-reader, like a Kobo, Amazon still makes great e-readers, and it's a testament to the brand that devices that are at least 14 years old are still going strong.</p> <p>Unfortunately, while a lot of Amazon devices get regular discounts throughout the year, we only see Kindle discounts during big shopping events like Prime Day and Black Friday. However, even at full price, these devices earn a strong recommendation from Mashable.</p> <p>Kindle Kids editions come with built-in parental controls. It displays a library geared toward kid-friendly and age-appropriate content. Plus, it allows your kid to request books to read that you have to approve in the Kindle app — that way, you know exactly what they're reading.</p> <p>Unlike other Kindle models, the Kindle Kids includes a built-in cover and a free one-year Amazon Kids+ subscription that provides access to thousands of books for kids ages 6 to 12. Plus, it comes with a worry-free guarantee: If the Kindle Kids gets damaged within two years of purchase, they'll replace it totally free.</p> <p>However, any Kindle can technically be a suitable device for kids. When setting up a Kindle, you'll receive a prompt asking if the device is for children, which enables parental controls on it.</p> <p>Yes, you can buy new Kindles at Best Buy. However, if you're searching for the best price and a convenient shopping experience, we recommend sticking with Amazon. Since the Kindle store is also hosted at Amazon, you'll need an account there anyway, and it's hard to beat Amazon's prices on its own products.</p> <p>To select the best Kindles of 2026, I assessed the current lineup of Kindle devices. I tested each of these e-readers to get a comprehensive view of each device. I also compared them to the other e-readers I've tested.</p> <p>I made sure to pinpoint which models are waterproof for a worry-free outdoor reading experience as well as those better suited for children.</p> <p>Kindle models vary wildly in price; I made sure to include an affordable Kindle as well as splurge-worthy, treat-yourself picks.</p> <p>This includes auto-adjusting displays for indoor/outdoor reading, warm light settings to block blue light, and page turn buttons for one-handed reading.</p> <p>How easy was it to download and read new books from Libby or the Amazon store? Did the Scribe's stylus work smoothly?</p> <p>Samantha Mangino, is an e-reader expert. She's tested all the Kindle and Kobo e-readers, diving into the buzziest models. Her love of reading extends beyond the e-reader beat, covering books and authors, too. In the tech space, she covers tablets, smart rings, and headphones.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/best-kindles-tested-nov-2026" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5243"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "The best iPads to buy in 2026: We tested every tablet in Apple’s lineup", "image": ["https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/roundups/04Naak7tKzKNOX1bWwQydhw/hero-image.fill.size_1200x675.v1795208783.jpg"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T11:00:00+00:00", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T11:00:00+00:00", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5243"}</script> <h2>The best iPads to buy in 2026: We tested every tablet in Apple’s lineup</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/best-ipads-tested-nov-2026" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/roundups/04Naak7tKzKNOX1bWwQydhw/hero-image.fill.size_1200x675.v1795208783.jpg" alt="The best iPads to buy in 2026: We tested every tablet in Apple’s lineup" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/roundups/04Naak7tKzKNOX1bWwQydhw/hero-image.fill.size_1200x675.v1795208783.jpg" alt="The best iPads to buy in 2026: We tested every tablet in Apple’s lineup" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Apple iPads are better than ever. We know because we've tested them all, from the older base model iPad to the brand-new iPad Mini. For shoppers suffering from decision fatigue, we can help you choose the right tablet for your budget and use case, whether that's drawing, playing games on the couch, getting some work done, or all of the above. So, based on extensive testing, we chose the best iPads of 2026.</p> <p>These are the tech, tools, and products — from laptops to e-readers, from earbuds to robovacs, and more — that Mashable ranks best in class.</p> <p>At this point, iPads can do almost anything your laptop can do. The new windowing and multitasking features introduced with Liquid Glass and iPadOS 26 (and recently updated in iPadOS 27) support a wide range of use cases. Not to mention, the iPad Air with M4 just got a memory boost up to 12GB, putting it a tier above many laptops. Whether you want to stream, play The Sims, browse the internet, illustrate, or edit videos, you can do it on the iPad.</p> <p>But which iPad should you choose? Apple's tablet lineup can be a bit confusing, especially with the recent release of the new iPad Mini, which saw a price increase to $699, almost as much as the iPad Air. To help you out, we've tested every single Apple tablet for sale in 2026, and we can recommend the right model for every type of Apple user.</p> <p>Yes, iPads will be on sale during Black Friday. In fact, they already are. So far, every single iPad is on sale, except for the new Mini. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile</p> <p>The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile</p> <p>The iPad Mini with A17 Pro chip is now on sale for just $499. That saves you $100 on the tablet, which we think is a great fit for readers and those who want a tablet for travel. However, our favorite tablet and iPad is the 11-inch iPad Air, which is also marked down. Grab it for $100 off during Black Friday, bringing it down to $649. And for budget shoppers, the older iPad with the A16 chip starts at $399, making it a very popular pick among Mashable readers.</p> <p>To find the latest Black Friday iPad deals, keep an eye on our Black Friday 2026 hub page.</p> <p>With the global memory crisis, iPad pricing took a hit. Apple raised prices across the board. Here's a breakdown of the price increases.</p> <p>Read our full review of the Apple iPad Air 2026 (M4).</p> <p>Pretty much everyone: The iPad Air is the best iPad for most people. It will be your new go-to device for streaming, reading, or creative projects. And if you plan on using your tablet as a substitute for a laptop, it'll work for that, too, especially when paired with the new Magic Keyboard for iPad Air or Apple Pencil. It's fast, moves smoothly between apps, and has a stunning high-definition display.</p> <p>Creatives on a budget: While we prefer the iPad Pro for creatives, the iPad Air is a more budget-friendly option that will still handle tough design jobs, and it's compatible with the Apple Pencil Pro.</p> <p>To kick off 2026, Apple upgraded the iPad Air to include the M4 chip. While the update didn't feel necessary in light of the already powerful iPad with M3 chip, we'll never say no to more computing power, especially with no price increase.</p> <p>The iPad Air bridges the gap between tablet and laptop, yet it still remains affordable and user-friendly. With the M4 chip upgrade, the iPad Air got a boost, now scoring 13,176 on the Geekbench 6 test, up from the previous model's score of 11,464.</p> <p>While the iPad Pro is technically slightly thinner and lighter, the iPad Air still lives up to its name. It's a great tablet to bring on the go due to its light weight, which helps avoid wrist strain when holding the device.</p> <p>It has crisp displays for brilliant graphics, and one of its most surprising upgrades in the 2026 model is more memory. Now with 12GB of RAM, it's better equipped as a gaming device, too. For creatives too, the extra memory makes it even more capable of creative tasks, whether that be in Final Cut Pro or while doing design work with the Apple Pencil.</p> <p>Essentially, it gives you everything you'd want in a starter MacBook, but it costs you just $749 and has a touchscreen.</p> <p>Budget shoppers: If you prefer saving money over splurging on the latest tech, the Apple iPad (11th generation) gets the job done. It's the base model, meaning it won't have the fastest processor like the iPad Air's M4 chip or the latest features like Apple Intelligence. However, it is the most affordable Apple tablet. With the M4 iPad Air and M5 iPad Pro models, this iPad's price dropped to $349 — a real steal for Apple.</p> <p>Even though its tech is basic compared to the advanced iPad Air and Pro, it's great for someone who wants a portable screen for streaming, playing games, and reading. It's also a nice choice if you're interested in using your tablet as a laptop, but don't want to spend a ton of money. It's compatible with the Magic Keyboard Folio and together costs $598 — the same price as the iPad Air.</p> <p>Kids: It's especially great for kids if you want to get them a tablet but don't want to spend a fortune on it.</p> <p>The iPad (11th generation) doesn't possess the flair of the Pro or Air, but it is a testament that Apple can still produce good entry-level tech. It's currently the lowest-priced iPad, even defeating the mini.</p> <p>The 2025 iPad model got a slight chip upgrade, finally going from the A14 up to the A16. While that's not quite as impressive as the M-series of chips, it is quick and powerful. On the Geekbench 6 test, it scored 6,233, falling quite a bit lower than the iPad Air and Pro. However, it still scored higher than most premium Chromebooks, so you can feel confident it can double as a workbook.</p> <p>It has a solid screen size of 10.9 inches, providing plenty of room for those who want to use it as a drawing pad or journal, since it is compatible with the Apple Pencil USB-C and Apple Pencil 1st generation.</p> <p>Overall, the iPad (11th generation) is a great entry-level tablet. It's best when working on a budget because if you're willing to spend a little more, the iPad Air is a better value.</p> <p>Read our full review of the iPad Mini (2026).</p> <p>Who is the iPad Mini for? That's a surprisingly hard question to answer. As our tech editor recently reviewed the newest Apple tablet and had this to say: "Let's cut to the chase: The iPad Mini is now my favorite tablet in the Apple lineup. It has an incredible display, excellent design, and strong performance. However, I'm not quite sure who should buy it."</p> <p>That's because the iPad Mini is best for entertainment and casual users, but it carries a very non-casual price tag, making it harder to recommend, even though it's an undeniably great product.</p> <p>Best for entertainment: If you're not interested in working on your iPad, then the iPad Mini is our top recommendation for you. Apple's mini tablet is now equipped with a gorgeous OLED display and a lighter, thinner design than the previous Mini. Even without an M-series chip, it still supports the new Siri AI and Apple Intelligence features. It's the perfect size for reading, and your Apple TV favorites will look fantastic on the OLED Super Retina XDR display, which boasts deep blacks and bright colors in all lighting conditions.</p> <p>Upper-income shoppers: The new iPad Mini has an incredible display and fits just right in your hands, and there are so many things we love about it. We don't love the price tag, however. The $699 price a really big ask for most shoppers, especially if you're just using it to read, browse, and stream. The relatively ancient base-model iPad can still do all those things, as can Android tablets and the new Alexa Pro tablets from Amazon — and at a much lower price.</p> <p>But if cost isn't a concern, then we think you'll love this compact tablet, the lightest tablet Apple has ever made.</p> <p>Mashable previously named the 7th-generation iPad Mini, released in October 2024, the best iPad for reading. And it's still available at Amazon for under $500, as of this writing. The 2026 iPad Mini has a steeper $699 price, equivalent to the MacBook Neo and only $50 less than the iPad Air, which has the M4 chip inside. But let's put aside the price for a moment: The new iPad Mini is an undeniably good tablet.</p> <p>Our tech editor said it has a "near-perfect design" that feels great in your hands. Apple also removed the speaker holes at the bottom of the design and introduced a new "Acoustic Display" system. Because of that, the newest iPad now has IP67 water and dust resistance. And the speakers sound great.</p> <p>The OLED display does go a long way in justifying the premium price. While it lacks the ProMotion Display technology found in the iPad Pro or MacBook Pro OLED, its peak and standard brightness are impressive. Even the littlest details on the screen are razor-sharp, and the colors/blacks pop off the screen.</p> <p>Read our full review of the Apple iPad Pro (M5).</p> <p>Professionals: Released in 2025, the current-gen iPad Pro is loaded with Apple's blazing-fast M5 chip, giving it way more power than the average user really needs. Graphic designers can store all of their creative work on the device, which has up to 2TB of storage. It's also a smart choice for video editors, though the premium nano-texture glass — which prevents glare while working in any environment, even full sun — will cost extra. Apple's legendary XDR Retina display, which combines super-bright tandem OLED with ProMotion technology, lets you play back HDR video with a high refresh rate.</p> <p>Photo and video editors: For creative professionals, the Apple iPad Pro is a dream come true. It's also compatible with the Apple Pencil Pro, a tool that features a unique squeezable design and a barrel roll feature, enhancing precision and control in design work. The XDR Retina Display is so good that the iPad Pro is essentially a reference-quality monitor you can take anywhere.</p> <p>While the iPad Air is going to be the best for most people, the iPad Pro levels things up for professionals.</p> <p>The 2025 iPad Pro isn't just a great tablet, but an outstanding laptop. Loaded with Apple's latest M5 processing chip, it's one of the most advanced options from Apple. When running the Geekbench 6 tests, it scored between 16,200 and 16,400. That puts it in the top 8 percent of all laptops we've tested. That's miraculous levels of power for such a sleek, razor-thin package.</p> <p>You can often find 1TB of storage in computers, but 2TB is rare in a laptop and nearly unheard of with a tablet, so it can store large files without a problem.</p> <p>Apple has released a lot of new tablets in the past two years, but for college students, we have to recommend the most affordable option — the base-model iPad with the A16 chip. It has a large 11-inch Liquid Retina Display, Touch ID, dual 12MP cameras for FaceTime calls, and just enough processing power to stream, read, take notes, and manage Word documents and Google Docs.</p> <p>The right iPad for you depends purely on your needs. Do you want something that has enough storage and speed for streaming? Do you want to use it to read? Do you want to use a tablet as your primary workstation? Your answers should factor into your decision.</p> <p>To get you started, we believe the best iPad for most people is the M4 iPad Air (2026). It has exceptional performance but is also in the middle range for both price and capability.</p> <p>That would be the iPad Air (M4), available in 11 or 13 inches. Combined with the Apple Pencil or Apple Pencil Pro, it serves as a very capable drawing tablet. Plus, the M4 processor is powerful enough to support the latest Apple Intelligence features in programs like Adobe Illustrator, Pixelmator, Photoshop, and Procreate.</p> <p>Amazon frequently offers discounts on Apple tablets, and you can also take advantage of the Apple Store's year-round education discounts. You can generally find the lowest prices on iPads during big shopping events such as Prime Day and Black Friday, though you can get lucky and catch discounts throughout the year. Black Friday is generally a smart time to buy, but given that Apple typically releases new iPads in October, the newest models may not be on sale for Black Friday-Cyber Monday.</p> <p>Yes, you can read on your iPad — no need for an additional e-reader. In fact, reading on your iPad makes traveling far more convenient. You can read and stream on one device rather than pack an e-reader that just lets you read.</p> <p>All iPads come pre-downloaded with Apple Books and Apple News to read books, magazines, and more. However, you can rely on something besides Apple's apps to read. If you're a Kindle convert or just want to use both, the Kindle app is available on iOS. And don't forget to support your local library and use Libby to gain access to endless audio and e-books.</p> <p>Even though Apple recently released the new iPad Mini (A20 Pro), we actually think the 7th-gen iPad Mini (A17 Pro) released in 2024 is the best iPad for readers, as it's significantly more affordable. During sales, you can find the previous-gen iPad Mini on sale for under $500, a much friendlier price for most readers and bibliophiles.</p> <p>The Mashable staff has tested and reviewed all of the iPads available today. We assessed each model for performance, usability, and special features.</p> <p>We looked at the specs of each product and how that measured up. Sure, the iPad Pro has the M5 chip, but does it outperform the iPad Air with the M3 chip? (Yes, and incredibly so.) We assessed its speed and processing through hands-on testing and Geekbench 6 tests.</p> <p>iPad displays are essential — they are a portable touchscreen. We watched videos to test displays and run creativity apps like ProCreate for accuracy and detail.</p> <p>Through daily use, we inspected the usability of these iPads. We assessed things like screen size, placement of front-facing cameras, and how useful special features were.</p> <p>With the release of the iPad Pro, we're assessing the special features available on these models, like the nano-texture glass and XDR display, to see if it's just added flair or actually valuable.</p> <p>Samantha Mangino, is an e-reader expert. She's tested all the Kindle and Kobo e-readers, diving into the buzziest models. Her love of reading extends beyond the e-reader beat, covering books and authors, too. In the tech space, she covers tablets, smart rings, and headphones.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/best-ipads-tested-nov-2026" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd;
