</script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.</p> <p>Bitcoin fell to a fresh bear market low as widespread selling picked up across crypto, stocks and gold.</p> <p>The Bitcoin sell-off deepened on Saturday, with the price reaching a low of $55,130, its lowest level since September 2024, extending a brutal run for risk assets. The latest sell-off deepens a bear market that began after Bitcoin peaked above $126,000 in October 2025.</p> <p>In an X post, Fidelity Investments' Director of Global Macro, Jurrien Timmer, says he remains bullish on Bitcoin based on its four-year cycle and Power Law math. Timmer isn't oblivious to Bitcoin's recent poor performance, which he highlighted in a separate X post.</p> <p>"I would be remiss if I didn’t mention Bitcoin and its very poor price action. It has gotten significantly more correlated to the S&P 500 in recent weeks, trading more like a speculative risk asset than a monetary asset," Timmer wrote.</p> <p>Timmer added that Bitcoin has a dual personality in his own view, consisting of a monetary factor and a speculative factor. He depicted this by showing the rate of change in the GS non-profitable tech basket and the global money supply. When both are expanding rapidly, Bitcoin moons, and vice versa. Right now, risk appetites are receding as the AI theme corrects, while global liquidity is also receding, Timmer explained.</p> <p>In his post, Timmer pointed out that Bitcoin is back on the Power Law support curve, which he says is a 'bit disheartening.' He highlighted the significance of this, saying that if Bitcoin’s price were ever to fall well below this support line, which is currently at $62,000 and rising, it would put his entire framework in doubt. "Never a dull moment!" he added.</p> <p>I remain bullish on Bitcoin based on its four-year cycle and Power Law math, but it’s a bit disheartening that we are back again sitting on that power law support curve. If Bitcoin’s price were to ever fall well below this support line (currently at $62k and rising), it would put my entire framework in doubt. Never a dull moment!</p> <p>Timmer's viewpoint seems to align with that of veteran trader Peter Brandt, who noted that Bitcoin might have entered a "dicey" price range owing to its recent drop. "The territory below this level gets very dicey," he said, underlining the $57,803 price point for Bitcoin.</p> <p>In September 2026, Timmer pointed to a potential double-bottom formation near $60,000, noting that breaking past resistance near $80,000–$86,000 would support a push toward $100,000. Timmer also projects Bitcoin could reach $300,000 by 2029 based on a power-law model.</p> <p>Bitcoin now trades well below $62,000, and this raises the question of whether the framework underpinning Timmer’s outlook still holds.</p> <p>Disclaimer: The opinions expressed here are not investment advice; they are provided for informational purposes only. The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. Every investment and all trading involves risk, so you should always perform your own research prior to making decisions. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. We do not recommend investing money you cannot afford to lose.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://u.today/never-a-dull-moment-fidelitys-timmer-raises-stakes-around-btcs-62000-price-level" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5343"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "Your AI agent has a wallet. Who answers for its decisions?", "image": ["https://fortune.com/img-assets/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/shlomit-wagman.jpg?resize=1200,600"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T11:00:00", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T11:00:00", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5343"}</script> <h2>Your AI agent has a wallet. Who answers for its decisions?</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://fortune.com/2026/11/21/chatgpt-wallet-ai-agents-payments-liability-mandates/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://fortune.com/img-assets/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/shlomit-wagman.jpg?resize=1200,600" alt="Your AI agent has a wallet. Who answers for its decisions?" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://fortune.com/img-assets/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/shlomit-wagman.jpg?resize=1200,600" alt="Your AI agent has a wallet. Who answers for its decisions?" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Last week, OpenAI launched ChatGPT Wallet, its second attempt at agentic payments after shelving in-chat checkout earlier this year. Its agents can now pay merchants on a user’s behalf, but every purchase still requires a human in the loop to approve it.</p> <p>That safeguard will not last, and it shouldn’t. An agent that needs your approval for every purchase is a faster checkout button, not an agent. Once approval moves from the transaction to the instruction, payments lose the assumption they were built on: that a human decides, at the moment of payment, that this is what they want.</p> <p>The industry is making rapid progress on how agents pay with existing payment methods. It has not yet solved what agents are allowed to decide, or who pays when they decide wrong. The companies that solve that problem will define the agentic economy.</p> <p>Imagine asking your agent to plan a family vacation within a $5,000 budget. It books nonrefundable flights on the wrong weekend, a hotel 40 minutes from the beach, and accepts a counteroffer from the airline’s own agent that quietly strips out checked bags.</p> <p>Every payment was authorized. Every merchant delivered exactly what was ordered. The trip is still nothing like what you asked for.</p> <p>OpenAI’s terms leave delivery and returns with merchants and preserve existing card dispute protections. However, agents introduce a different kind of failure: a purchase that was properly authorized and fulfilled, but did not reflect the user’s instructions. Existing dispute mechanisms may offer remedies, but they do not clearly establish who should bear the loss when the failure lies in the agent’s judgment.</p> <p>Authorization to spend is not authorization to judge. Today’s infrastructure only knows how to verify the first.</p> <p>The industry is beginning to build verifiable mandates. Google’s Agent Payments Protocol, for example, uses cryptographically signed mandates to record what a user asked for and approved. That is necessary, but not sufficient. A signature can prove what was authorized. It cannot prove that the agent interpreted the user’s preferences correctly, acted in their interest, or was free of outside incentives. Closing that gap requires a broader idea of what the industry has started calling Know Your Agent. Today it means verifying whose agent is acting and what it was authorized to do. It must also mean verifying whether the agent exercised that authority as intended. That requires three layers.</p> <p>First, mandates that capture intent. In practice, agent permissions still center on price limits, timing and product parameters. A meaningful mandate also encodes intent: the user’s priorities, the trade-offs they accept and reject, and the conditions that require escalation, such as “nothing nonrefundable without asking me.” Relevant constraints must be verifiable across the transaction, by the merchant’s agent, the payment provider and any later arbiter, without unnecessarily exposing the user’s private instructions. A spending limit tells the system how much. A mandate tells it what for.</p> <p>Second, a liability framework. When an agent acts inside its spending authority but outside its mandate, the loss could land in many places: the user who gave an ambiguous instruction, the provider whose model misread it, the merchant whose agent extracted worse terms, the platform that ranked the options, or the payment provider that executed the transaction. No rule decides among them today, so every failure is likely to become a dispute. To earn consumers’ trust, the allocation should be settled in advance, for example by asking which party can prevent the harm at the lowest cost. Other industries have done this. The card industry’s EMV liability shift made the party that had not adopted chip technology bear certain counterfeit-fraud losses, creating a powerful incentive to upgrade. Product liability law allocates defects along the supply chain. Clear allocation also creates a market: risk that can be assigned can be priced and insured.</p> <p>Third, a duty of loyalty. OpenAI is not taking a cut of Wallet transactions at launch. But business models evolve. Once an agent’s platform earns money from merchants, through fees, paid placement or advertising, the agent faces a conflict of interest its user cannot see. Financial regulation addressed a similar conflict for brokers and advisers through disclosure and best-interest obligations. Consumer agents need enforceable standards for whose interests they serve, how paid influence is disclosed, and how compliance with the user’s mandate can be independently verified.</p> <p>AI labs, payment companies and commerce platforms are writing the agentic protocols right now. Private standards are well suited to identity, credentials and payment flows, because every participant benefits from interoperability. They are structurally weak on liability, because every party at the table would prefer the loss to land somewhere else.</p> <p>I have worked on both sides of the rulebook, as chair of the FATF’s Risk Group and as a fintech executive. In both roles, the rules that worked were the ones that settled, in advance, who absorbs failure. Systems that answer that question scale. Systems that leave it open stall, or have the answer imposed by regulators after a visible failure, usually in terms written for the last crisis.</p> <p>The companies that build mandates, clear liability rules and loyalty into their agents now will not be slowed down by it. They will be the ones consumers trust with the wallet. Card payments became global infrastructure because people believed that when something went wrong, someone would make it right. Agents need the same promise, across consumers, AI platforms, merchants and financial institutions that must trust decisions none of them fully controls.</p> <p>Whoever makes that promise credible will do more than enable agentic payments. They will help write the rules of the new economy.</p> <p>The opinions expressed in Fortune.com commentary pieces are solely the views of their authors and do not necessarily reflect the opinions and beliefs of Fortune.</p> <p>Dr. Shlomit Wagman is a fellow at the Mossavar-Rahmani Center for Business and Government at Harvard Kennedy School and founder of Integria AI, focused on AI policy in finance and agentic payments. She previously chaired the FATF Risk Group, led Israel’s Money Laundering and Terror Financing Prohibition Authority, and held C-level roles in the payments industry. She holds a Ph.D. and LL.M. from Yale Law School and is the author of the forthcoming book “Synthetic Agents, Real Money.”</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://fortune.com/2026/11/21/chatgpt-wallet-ai-agents-payments-liability-mandates/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5344"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "GoFundMe CEO sees ‘new era of desperation’ after 40% spike in essentials fundraisers", "image": ["https://fortune.com/img-assets/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/GettyImages-1715991736-e1795213654503.jpg?resize=1200,600"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T11:00:00", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T11:00:00", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5344"}</script> <h2>GoFundMe CEO sees ‘new era of desperation’ after 40% spike in essentials fundraisers</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://fortune.com/2026/11/21/gofundme-fundraisers-for-groceries-essentials-increased-40-percent-american-affordability-crisis/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://fortune.com/img-assets/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/GettyImages-1715991736-e1795213654503.jpg?resize=1200,600" alt="GoFundMe CEO sees ‘new era of desperation’ after 40% spike in essentials fundraisers" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://fortune.com/img-assets/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/GettyImages-1715991736-e1795213654503.jpg?resize=1200,600" alt="GoFundMe CEO sees ‘new era of desperation’ after 40% spike in essentials fundraisers" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>As more Americans struggle to put food on the table, crowdsourcing money for groceries has become consumers’ balm to an ongoing affordability crisis.</p> <p>Food-related GoFundMe fundraisers have increased 35% year-over-year in the platform’s English-speaking markets of the U.S., Canada, UK, Australia, and Ireland, including a 40% jump in the U.S., according to the company. The amount of money given to these campaigns increased 50% since the year before.</p> <p>“The costs of regular things—food, rent, gas prices, obviously, particularly this year, and not just here but elsewhere in the world—have gone up, and folks are struggling in many cases to be able to afford all of that,” CEO Tim Cadogan told Fortune. “Things are more expensive, and a lot of people’s incomes have not moved up at a commensurate rate.”</p> <p>This year’s growing fundraising demand for essentials is part of a larger trend that began during the pandemic, when the intersection of widespread job losses,health emergencies, and a transition to virtual community led more people to seek help online.</p> <p>GoFundMe introduced the food, rent, and monthly bills tab in October 2020 in response to an increased need for necessities during the pandemic. Martin Lukk, a postdoctoral research fellow at the Data Sciences Institute at the University of Toronto, said while the company created the new category as a result of increased demand, it also was a catalyst for even more people seeking help through the platform.</p> <p>“In response to an overwhelming number of campaigns for exactly these things, they invented this new category, and that seemed to usher in this new era of desperation that probably really didn’t exist during the pandemic,” Lukk told Fortune.</p> <p>Fundraisers for essentials also increased last year, when the platform saw a 20% rise in the number of fundraisers to cover essentials from rent to utilities to groceries, which quadrupled the year before. The “monthly bills” category on the platform was among the fastest-growing, second only to individual support for nonprofits.</p> <p>“We have been seeing that trend now really for about four years, really since the late stage of the pandemic had a lot of impacts on supply chains that’s flowed through,” Cadogan said.</p> <p>There are also variables outside the economy that may have contributed to the shift toward more necessity-related campaigns, including more people simply knowing about GoFundMe.</p> <p>Crowdsourced fundraising was documented as early as the 1700s, when the Irish Loan Fund gave small loans to low-income rural families who would pool resources together. The U.S. saw perhaps its most famous crowdfunding campaign in 1885, when publisher and lawmaker Joseph Pulitzer launched an effort to raise money for the pedestal on which the Statue of Liberty would rest. More than 160,000 donors would raise $100,000 in about five months. But the rise of GoFundMe, which was founded in 2010, coincided with the financial crisis tightening lending requirements, forcing artists and entrepreneurs to turn to the internet to raise money. Ventures soon expanded from creative endeavors to philanthropic initiatives and charity drives.</p> <p>This year’s increase in fundraising correlates with ongoing affordability woes stoked specifically by the Iran war and tariff uncertainty. Americans now spend nearly half their income on food and housing alone, according to the U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS)’s annual Consumer Expenditures Report published last month. Compared to the year before, food spending increased 4.6%, and housing expenditures climbed 5.8%, with the share of spending on these necessities reaching its highest in 15 years.</p> <p>While much of that increase is related to rising costs, it’s also a result of stagnating wages. Minutes from the latest Federal Open Market Committee meeting found wage growth for most industries was lagging behind the inflation rate of 3.7% over the last 12 months. According to BLS data, the average wage growth in the U.S. over the last 12 months ending in September 2026 was around 3.1%. Inflation-adjusted wages and salaries fell 0.6% over the same period.</p> <p>Economic pressures tend to mount around the holidays. Walmart CEO John Furner told investors on Thursday that customers are “stable, resilient,” ahead of the holiday season, but according to the company, about half of Americans say their finances are the greatest stressor as they are expected to serve large holiday meals and buy presents for loved ones.</p> <p>Just as widespread factors like a pandemic and economic tensions can spur crowdsourcing, other variables like political shifts can also impact fundraising demand. Lukk posited that with a wave of Democratic lawmakers moving to Washington, there’s greater opportunities for policy that could eventually alleviate the economic pressures that cause people to turn to GoFundMe campaigns. Republicans largely conceded that they lost so many midterm elections because they failed to address Americans’ affordability concerns.</p> <p>“I wouldn’t be surprised if, with greater leverage in the Senate and the House, that any kind of social policy initiatives the Democrats might be able to pass in the coming years, that those might be able to absorb some of this, and potentially reduce the the amount of campaigns that we see on GoFundMe,” Lukk told Fortune.</p> <p>In his book GoFailMe: The Unfulfilled Promise of Digital Crowdfunding written alongside Erik Schneiderhan, Lukk explains that in the Canadian province of Quebec, where there are more social programs, crowdfunding is less prevalent than in provinces with fewer social safety net programs.</p> <p>GoFundMe has seen a similar pattern in its own data. Between November 2025 and 2026, food-related fundraisers in the U.S. have actually plummeted by about 20%, a trend the company attributes to the Trump administration freezing SNAP benefits last November, which caused a spike in food-related campaigns on the platform.</p> <p>According to Lukk, GoFundMe is unlikely to see a reduction in campaigns as the platform continues to grow, but rather, there’s an opportunity for less need for people to start fundraisers if there are changes to social policy.</p> <p>“GoFundMe really seems to respond to those gaps in the safety net. So, if there were a kind of systemic framework to think about what leads people to GoFundMe and what increases the kind of pressure on those fundraisers, it’s about where the gaps in safety net might be coming up, where the net might be fraying, and where potentially future policy interventions might be able to repair some of that,” Lukk said.</p> <p>“I wouldn’t give up on social policies,” he added, “and at least to continue imagining a world where some of these could reduce the kind of desperation that we see flowing out onto GoFundMe.”</p> <p>Sasha Rogelberg is a reporter and former editorial fellow on the news desk at Fortune, covering retail and the intersection of business and popular culture.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://fortune.com/2026/11/21/gofundme-fundraisers-for-groceries-essentials-increased-40-percent-american-affordability-crisis/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5239"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "'Accept cookies'? The revelation has rattled the Nordic nation, whose proximity to Russia and unusually open public records system make it particularly vulnerable to Moscow’s hybrid war tactics.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://www.france24.com/en/europe/20261121-accept-cookies-russia-collected-data-swaths-unknowing-swedes" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://www.cnet.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/2/73108-hyundaielantra-208.jpg" alt="New Hyundai Elantra Hybrid Debuts With 53 Mpg, AI in the Dash" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.</p> <p>Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.</p> <p>The redesigned compact sedan features Hyundai’s new AI voice assistant, Gleo AI, and an updated regenerative braking system with EV-like one-pedal driving.</p> <p>Hyundai pulled back the cover on the new 2027 Elantra sedan and 2028 Elantra Hybrid this week at the LA Auto Show, and, wow, it looks like nothing else in its class. The bold styling may not be for everyone — though, personally, I dig it — but economy car shoppers will certainly be interested in the compact hybrid sedan’s targeted 53 mpg and Gleo, Hyundai’s new AI voice assistant that controls the automaker’s new Android Automotive OS-based dashboard tech.</p> <p>The humble Hyundai Elantra hasn’t historically been a sexy car, but it remains an important one for the brand. It’s the automaker’s second-bestselling vehicle, behind only the Tucson. More importantly, the Elantra is one of the stalwarts of the affordable compact sedan class, a segment that many US automakers have abandoned in favor of more profitable SUVs. At a time when buyers are more sensitive than ever to inflated fuel costs and sticker prices, economy car options like the Elantra and its second-generation hybrid powertrain are worth our attention.</p> <p>The updated hybrid powertrain pairs a 1.6-liter four-cylinder gasoline engine with a 6-speed dual-clutch automatic gearbox. Combined output at the front wheels rises to 155 horsepower (up from 139 hp in the current car), thanks to a more powerful 45-kilowatt electric-assist motor (up from 32kW).</p> <p>An updated regenerative braking system recaptures energy when slowing and now offers an “EV-like one-pedal driving experience” that can bring the car to a complete stop at its maximum setting. Additionally, the hybrid now features a new Stay mode that lets the climate control and infotainment systems run while parked for up to 30 minutes without firing up the gas engine.</p> <p>Sharing the stage in LA, the new nonhybrid Elantra arrives with the same 2.0-liter four-cylinder engine and continuously variable transmission as the current generation. At 147 hp with a target of 35 mpg combined, it’s no more powerful than before, but it should also be no less efficient, despite the sedan’s increased size. I’d say the hybrid is the better choice, though it will likely also be the more expensive powertrain option.</p> <p>Inside, a new cockpit design moves the now-hoodless digital instrument cluster, a 9.9-inch “Slim Information Display,” away from the driver and toward the windshield on the dashboard. The new placement, similar to what I’ve seen on recent Toyota models, puts vehicle information closer to the driver’s line of sight.</p> <p>Pleos Connect, Hyundai’s new Android Automotive OS-based infotainment suite, lives on a 12.9-inch center display. Despite being based on Android, it still features the automaker’s bespoke navigation and media software, rather than Google Maps. The system is said to be over-the-air updatable and features standard wireless Apple CarPlay and Android Auto for drivers who’d rather bring their own apps on the road.</p> <p>The Pleos dashboard OS is matched with Gleo AI (who names these things?!), which is Hyundai’s new AI voice assistant that lets drivers and passengers talk to the car rather than occupy a hand on the touchscreen. Hyundai says the system features “contextual language support, multi-command capability and zone-specific recognition,” which basically means the car knows who’s talking to it and which seat they’re in and can assign context to voice prompts based on the situation.</p> <p>“Affordable doesn’t have to be anonymous,” said Olabisi Boyle, Hyundai’s senior vice president of product, at the unveiling. I agree: Hyundai’s designers clearly aren’t afraid to push the design envelope, and the new Elantra is anything but bland.</p> <p>The sedan introduces the automaker’s Art of Steel design language, with chunky flared fenders that feel inspired by the brand’s recent new SUVs. The tall greenhouse looks a bit cartoonish if you catch it at the wrong angle, but it provides increased headroom inside. From certain angles, the sedan’s beefy fenders remind me of an angular Korean take on the Subaru WRX. From other viewpoints, I can see inspiration from Hyundai’s N Vision 74 concept and the hydrogen-fueled Nexo, particularly in the triangular rear windows.</p> <p>The sedan measures 188.8 inches bumper-to-bumper on a 108.3-inch wheelbase that is 1.2 inches longer than the current model’s. Hyundai says the stretch for this generation improves rear-passenger space, now rated at 39.1 inches of legroom on the second row. The Elantra, according to its designers, now approaches the interior volume of a midsize car, a claim I’d take with a grain of salt had I not watched multiple six-foot-plus attendees slip into the back seat with relative ease.</p> <p>Hyundai did not announce pricing or detailed specs at the auto show debut, and availability is still vague. However, based on the stated model years, which are usually slightly offset from calendar years, I expect the 2027 Elantra sedan to arrive in early 2027, with the 2028 Elantra Hybrid following in late 2027.</p> <p>Editors’ note: Antuan Goodwin’s travel costs were covered by Genesis. The judgments and opinions of CNET are our own.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://www.cnet.com/roadshow/automobiles/2028-hyundai-elantra-hybrid-sedan-ai-assistant-la-auto-show/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5307"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "S&P 500 correction: Five forces behind the market sell-off", "image": ["https://image.cnbcfm.com/api/v1/image/108554307-17950192062026-11-18t162223z_189106541_rc2dqoahnid0_rtrmadp_0_usa-stocks.jpeg?v=1795019320&w=1920&h=1080"], "datePublished": "2026-11-20T23:53:21+0000", "dateModified": "2026-11-20T23:53:21+0000", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5307"}</script> <h2>S&P 500 correction: Five forces behind the market sell-off</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 2026-11-20  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://www.cnbc.com/2026/11/20/sp-500-correction-market-sell-off.html" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://image.cnbcfm.com/api/v1/image/108554307-17950192062026-11-18t162223z_189106541_rc2dqoahnid0_rtrmadp_0_usa-stocks.jpeg?v=1795019320&w=1920&h=1080" alt="S&P 500 correction: Five forces behind the market sell-off" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://image.cnbcfm.com/api/v1/image/108554307-17950192062026-11-18t162223z_189106541_rc2dqoahnid0_rtrmadp_0_usa-stocks.jpeg?v=1795019320&w=1920&h=1080" alt="S&P 500 correction: Five forces behind the market sell-off" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Esteemed Wharton professor Jeremy Siegel was more animated than usual on Friday afternoon... with good reason.</p> <p>The normally staid author of "Stocks for the Long Run" spoke to CNBC on Friday as the market careened toward some ugly milestones. The S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average closed in correction territory, off more than 10% from their records. The Nasdaq Composite lost its gain for the year and the more concentrated Nasdaq-100 entered bear market territory on an intraday basis.</p> <p>"These things are like straws that break the camel's back. You know what I mean? Everyone [is] all, 'I don't care, I don't care cause everything else is good' and when those straws keep on going... Bang," said Siegel.</p> <p>Just a month-and-a-half ago, the S&P 500 and the Nasdaq Composite were at record highs. But the straws were already adding up by then and a market in denial is just now recognizing them.</p> <p>Here are the reasons behind the sell-off:</p> <p>The breaking point came this week in the form of the initial public offering of artificial intelligence leader Anthropic and a pick-up in the selling intensity for one-time AI chip leader Nvidia.</p> <p>Anthropic upped its offering amount a day before going public despite the already dimming climate around AI-related stocks. The move ensured it would be the biggest IPO ever, but played into the fears that this debut — following SpaceX's monster offering over the summer — was a collective cash-out moment for the AI complex that will leave the investing masses holding the bag.</p> <p>In other words, it could be the ultimate "sell the news" moment for the AI trade.</p> <p>On Thursday, Anthropic traded from its $83 IPO price to a high above $115 before instantly rolling over and taking the AI trade with it. Anthropic's sell-off intensified Friday, and when the dust settled Anthropic closed the week at $88.91.</p> <p>The Nasdaq posted its worst week since the Covid plunge of March 2020, down 10%.</p> <p>Nvidia fell 14% on Friday with the selling intensifying after a Reuters headline hit the tape that the chipmaker's accounting is the focus of a Securities and Exchange Commission probe.</p> <p>Nvidia is now down nearly 40% in November alone, a month that saw the AI bull market's leading salesman, Jensen Huang, resign as its CEO because of an affair with an alleged Chinese spy that raised speculation about whether the company's chips were ending up in Beijing's hands despite government restrictions.</p> <p>To start the week, the Commerce Department tightened restrictions on U.S.-made chips to stop China from accessing them remotely from other countries.</p> <p>A solid earnings report on Tuesday fell flat as Nvidia's board definitively ruled out any chance Huang would return, disappointing some of the stock's fanatics still holding out hope for his far-fetched return. Nvidia ended the week with a 23% loss.</p> <p>The S&P 500 traded below its 200-day moving average — what's considered a measure of a security's long-term trend — on Tuesday and made a convincing break of it on Thursday. This turned many on Wall Street— like commodity trading advisors — into automatic sellers on the market. So-called CTAs are individuals or organizations that advise others on trading futures and swaps across a range of assets.</p> <p>"We know there's a lot of trend riders on these markets," said Siegel. "They have their trend lines. They've ridden it up and as soon as it goes down, they're selling and they're selling and they're selling and they just perpetuate" the decline, he said.</p> <p>The Iran conflict has caused the price oil to surge and while it's off its highs it still remains above $83 a barrel, while the fees for transiting the Strait of Hormuz and the rest of the region keep persistent pressure on global prices.</p> <p>A series of numbers earlier in the month showed inflation is still far from the Federal Reserve's 2% target, sparking a run of central bank officials to warn they may have to get more aggressive with interest rates.</p> <p>After increasing rates in September by a quarter point, the Fed skipped a hike in late October and sparked concern among bond investors it may be falling behind the inflation fight. The 10-year Treasury yield subsequently jumped above 5% and stayed there until a flight of safe haven fixed income buying brought it back below that level on Friday.</p> <p>Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller said this week that the Fed may need to do a half percentage point hike next month to catch up.</p> <p>"I'm very, very concerned about inflation. It's kind of headed in the wrong direction, and it's spreading out," Waller told CNBC.</p> <p>"Don't fight the Fed" has long been a maxim on Wall Street and right now expectations are that the central bank will need to raise rates in December and again in January.</p> <p>And late-breaking on Friday was a report from the Financial Times that the Trump Administration is considering restarting strikes on Iran.</p> <p>Before the grim market milestones were met on Friday, it had long lost its mojo on fears that the pace of growth driven by AI companies was slowing.</p> <p>As Anthropic prepared to go public, its third-quarter figures released earlier this month showed revenue growth last quarter was 47%, down from 144% in the second quarter.</p> <p>AI momentum trades, like the VanEck Semiconductor ETF and the iShares MSCI USA Momentum Factor ETF, all topped out during the summer.</p> <p>The fears of slowing growth picked up this month after the Democrats won control of Congress and key governorships, fueling speculation that states — or even the whole country — could implement stricter rules on data center construction.</p> <p>CoreWeave, which builds data centers using high amounts of debt, is down 71% from its high. Caterpillar, which has reinvented itself as an AI play because of data center construction and generators, is down more than 50% from its high.</p> <p>The view of Siegel and many market observers is that the economy remains in good enough shape that this will just be a run-of-the-mill correction that resets a bull market that had raised people's expectations too high because of its early ferocity.</p> <p>The S&P 500 returned more than 20% in 2023 and 2024 and then gained 16% last year.</p> <p>"The average over long periods for the stock market is 8%, 9% a year up," said Siegel. "People began to think that 15 to 20 [percent] was going to be the norm with AI and we have to bring that down to reality."</p> <p>The only problem with that theory is that the stock market and its wealth effect was one of the major factors underpinning the economy. Stocks are a greater source of household wealth — especially for baby boomers — than they ever have been before.</p> <p>With the S&P 500 clinging to a 1% gain for the year, this stock market rout could end up tipping off a virtuous cycle that slows the economy, and in turn, leads to bigger equity losses.</p> <p>Got a confidential news tip? </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>When using the out-of-the-box Gemini Enterprise web application, identity and access management happen behind the scenes. Employees sign in through Google Identity or Workforce Identity Federation (such as Microsoft Entra ID or Okta), search results automatically respect per-user access controls (ACLs), and third-party login prompts for Federated Connectors or custom agents appear automatically in the chat UI.</p> <p>In practice, however, many enterprises build their own Customer UI to embed Gemini Enterprise into an internal portal, intranet, or line-of-business application. To do this, Customer BE calls the Gemini Enterprise StreamAssist API.</p> <p>The moment an application builds a custom UI and calls the StreamAssist API directly, the three authentication flows that the built-in web app handled automatically become the responsibility of Customer UI and Customer BE:</p> <p>This guide walks through the architecture, component flows, and runnable code for all three layers (Figure 1):</p> <p>Depending on whether Customer UI is acting on behalf of an individual employee or running as a shared backend bot, customer architectures follow three keyless authentication patterns:</p> <p>Required IAM Role and License: Every principal calling the StreamAssist API must be granted the Discovery Engine User IAM role on the GCP project. For human user patterns (Pattern 1A and Pattern 1B), the user (or Workforce Pool group) must also have an active Gemini Enterprise user license assigned in the Admin Console.</p> <p>When employees sign in with Google Workspace or Cloud Identity and the Gemini Enterprise engine is configured for Google Identity, the flow has zero extra hops:</p> <p>When employees sign into Customer UI with Microsoft Entra ID or Okta and Gemini Enterprise uses Workforce Identity Federation (WIF), you reuse the same Workforce Pool that is already connected to Gemini Enterprise:</p> <p>When a backend service, Slack/Teams webhook, or non-personalized knowledge bot needs to call StreamAssist without any Service Account JSON keys, use a project-scoped Workload Identity Pool:</p> <p>When to use Pattern 1C vs. Patterns 1A and 1B: Because Workload Identity Federation authenticates a machine rather than an individual human employee, Gemini Enterprise sees every request as coming from the same workload identity. Use Pattern 1C for non-personalized bots over shared knowledge bases, and use Pattern 1A or Pattern 1B whenever you need per-user document ACLs, user-specific 3LO connectors, or restricted custom agents.</p> <p>Once Customer UI and Customer BE are calling StreamAssist as the signed-in employee, users can query native Federated Connectors (such as Google Drive, Microsoft SharePoint Online, Jira, Box, ServiceNow, and Confluence) that search live third-party APIs in real time.</p> <p>Because Federated Connectors enforce user-level permissions in the third-party system, each employee authorizes the connector once using 3-Legged OAuth (3LO). Gemini Enterprise stores those per-user refresh tokens inside its own managed Keystore, so Customer BE never has to store third-party credentials in its own database.</p> <p>Note for Custom UIs: Enabling Automatic Popup Handoff from GE FE Server: All Gemini Enterprise Federated Connectors use Google's managed /oauth-redirect endpoint on GE FE Server as their fixed callback URL. By default, that page only sends browser postMessage events to the built-in Gemini Enterprise web app origin. When deploying Customer UI on a custom domain, work with your Gemini Enterprise team to enable the custom origin flag so Step 7 automatically posts the callback URL to Customer UI and closes the popup.</p> <p>Native Federated Connectors are ideal for federated search and built-in actions. When a workflow requires multi-step reasoning, custom Python logic, or orchestrating across multiple enterprise APIs in a single assistant, teams build a custom ADK agent, deploy it on Vertex AI Agent Engine, and register it in Gemini Enterprise.</p> <p>When registering an ADK agent in Gemini Enterprise, attaching static OAuth configurations directly to the root agent registration creates two issues for a custom UI:</p> <p>To trigger 3-Legged OAuth on demand per tool and let Gemini Enterprise and Google Cloud manage the per-user Token Vault automatically, follow three simple setup steps:</p> <p>We have packaged all three authentication layers, along with interactive web test UIs, CLI scripts, and curl commands into the gemini-enterprise-custom-ui-auth GitHub repository:</p> <p>Agent Anomaly Detection: Safeguarding agents across other clouds</p> <p>How to securely connect internal Cloud Run MCP servers to Gemini Enterprise</p> <p>Check what your AI agents do before they do it, with Semantic governance policies</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://developers.googleblog.com/how-to-handle-user-connector-and-agent-auth-in-gemini-enterprise-with-a-custom-ui/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5236"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "Check what your AI agents do before they do it, with Semantic governance policies", "image": ["https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-developer-goog-blog-assets/images/header_2.2e16d0ba.fill-1200x600.png"], "datePublished": "2026-11-20T22:59:11.192456", "dateModified": "2026-11-20T22:59:11.192456", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5236"}</script> <h2>Check what your AI agents do before they do it, with Semantic governance policies</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://developers.googleblog.com/check-what-your-ai-agents-do-before-they-do-it-with-semantic-governance-policies/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-developer-goog-blog-assets/images/header_2.2e16d0ba.fill-1200x600.png" alt="Check what your AI agents do before they do it, with Semantic governance policies" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-developer-goog-blog-assets/images/header_2.2e16d0ba.fill-1200x600.png" alt="Check what your AI agents do before they do it, with Semantic governance policies" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Two days before earnings, an employee asks their coworker agent to send the preliminary quarterly results to an investor who says they're under NDA, and to copy the employee's personal Gmail so they can review the numbers tonight. The agent can read the file and send email, so it fulfills the user request. Semantic governance policies, available in Preview in Gemini platform, enable enterprises to govern their agents and provide guardrails to stop that call before it runs. They check each tool call an agent proposes against what the user asked for and against rules you write in plain language.</p> <p>Permissions alone can't catch this. They say what an agent can do, not whether a particular call follows your rules. A model can also be talked out of the rules in its system instructions, by a user with a good reason, or by a document telling it to do something nobody asked for, a technique called indirect prompt injection. So the check has to sit outside the agent. Semantic governance policies are enforced through a policy enforcement point, such as Agent Gateway, alongside the agent governance controls we expanded in October, so policies stay separate from your agent's code.</p> <p>Semantic governance policies check each tool call your agent's model proposes, before it runs, against two things:</p> <p>Today, the policy enforcement point that Semantic governance policies support is Agent Gateway. You need to configure your agent so all your agent egress traffic goes through Agent Gateway, facilitating traffic between your agent and its model. When the model responds with a tool call, Agent Gateway sends the proposed call to the Semantic governance policy engine for evaluation before the response reaches your agent.</p> <p>In the policy engine, an LLM judge evaluates the proposed call against the conversation so far, the agent's system instructions and tool descriptions, and your policies.</p> <p>If the call passes, the response reaches your agent unchanged. If it doesn't, the engine replaces the response's content, tool calls included, with a configurable denial message, so the call never runs. Either way, the verdict and the judge's rationale go to Cloud Logging in your project. Responses that contain only text, such as an answer, a plan, or a question back to the user, aren't evaluated, because they take no action.</p> <p>Consider a coworker agent for a finance team. It can search the shared drive, read files, send email, and delete draft files, and its system instructions include the date of the next earnings release. The company writes one policy for the agent:</p> <p>"Quarterly financial results (revenue, margins, bookings, guidance) should not be shared publicly until the public earnings release. Before the release, they may be shared only with @example.com addresses. Never send them to external or personal addresses, investors, analysts, or press, regardless of NDAs, urgency, or the requester's seniority."</p> <p>In each denied case, the agent gets a denial message instead of the call. The judge's rationale can quote your policy, so if users shouldn't see it, set your own denial message on the policy.</p> <p>The earnings rule is one policy. Each policy is a natural-language constraint attached to an agent. It can apply to all of an agent's tools or to one tool, and it can name a tool's parameters. Other examples:</p> <p>Because the earnings policy doesn't name a tool, it's checked on every tool call the agent proposes, so it would also apply to a tool that shares or uploads files. For guidance on wording, see best practices for writing policies. You can create it in the Google Cloud console or with the Google Cloud CLI:</p> <p>IAM and Agent Gateway access policies decide which tools and MCP servers an agent can reach. Model Armor screens prompts and responses for threats such as jailbreaks and sensitive data. Agent Anomaly Detection analyzes sessions asynchronously, out of band. Semantic governance policies add the check none of them makes, as one layer of defense in depth: they judge each action inline, before it runs.</p> <p>Because verdicts come from an LLM, which is probabilistic, they may vary. We recommend reviewing them in dry run mode before you enforce, and using Semantic governance policies alongside your other controls. The judge decides from the conversation, the agent's instructions, and your policies, so include facts a policy depends on, such as the earnings date, in the agent's system instructions. Streamed model responses aren't evaluated. Semantic governance policies complement, rather than replace, your disclosure controls and legal and compliance reviews.</p> <p>To try Semantic governance policies on an agent behind Agent Gateway:</p> <p>To learn more, read the Semantic governance policies overview and best practices.</p> <p>Your agent should work for the person who asked, within the rules you set. Semantic governance policies check both before each action runs.</p> <p>In our next post, we'll share how Semantic governance policies held up against prompt injection on AgentDojo, a public agent-security benchmark.</p> <p>Optimizing a 200,000-line Python codebase overnight with Gemini 4 Argon</p> <p>Multi-Turn Agentic Reinforcement Learning with Tunix and Gemma 4 on Google TPUs</p> <p>Agent Anomaly Detection: Safeguarding agents across other clouds</p> <p>Accelerating development for massive Git repositories</p> <p>How we built the Google I/O 2026 Save the Date experience</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://developers.googleblog.com/check-what-your-ai-agents-do-before-they-do-it-with-semantic-governance-policies/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5235"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "How to securely connect internal Cloud Run MCP servers to Gemini Enterprise", "image": ["https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-developer-goog-blog-assets/images/header_image_1600x900.2e16d0ba.fill-1200x600.png"], "datePublished": "2026-11-20T22:59:10.001341", "dateModified": "2026-11-20T22:59:10.001341", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5235"}</script> <h2>How to securely connect internal Cloud Run MCP servers to Gemini Enterprise</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://developers.googleblog.com/how-to-securely-connect-internal-cloud-run-mcp-servers-to-gemini-enterprise/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-developer-goog-blog-assets/images/header_image_1600x900.2e16d0ba.fill-1200x600.png" alt="How to securely connect internal Cloud Run MCP servers to Gemini Enterprise" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-developer-goog-blog-assets/images/header_image_1600x900.2e16d0ba.fill-1200x600.png" alt="How to securely connect internal Cloud Run MCP servers to Gemini Enterprise" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Enterprise teams adopting Gemini Enterprise are increasingly building custom Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers to connect AI agents with internal APIs, databases, and business workflows. Google Cloud Run is a natural fit for hosting these lightweight StreamableHTTP MCP servers: it scales automatically, manages TLS certificates out of the box, and integrates natively with Cloud IAM.</p> <p>However, moving a custom MCP server from an initial prototype to an enterprise production environment immediately introduces three real-world security and networking challenges:</p> <p>Here is how you can solve all three challenges using Egress Agent Gateway, Private Service Connect (PSC), and FQDN-scoped Private Cloud DNS, complete with a runnable reference implementation and setup scripts on GitHub.</p> <p>Connecting Gemini Enterprise (which runs in a Google-managed tenant project) to an internal serverless Cloud Run MCP server requires bridging two network hops and one scoped DNS lookup:</p> <p>Three Google Cloud networking and identity mechanisms work together in this architecture:</p> <p>Unlike a Compute Engine VM with an internal IP address inside your subnet, Cloud Run is serverless. Private routing therefore uses two bridges:</p> <p>When the Egress Agent Gateway receives a tool call for your Cloud Run MCP server URL, it resolves the hostname once before injecting the packet into your VPC. Without Private Cloud DNS, public DNS resolves .run.app domains to public internet IPs, which time out (HTTP 502) because a PSC Network Attachment does not route out to the public internet.</p> <p>Instead of creating a wildcard *.run.app private zone, which would override DNS resolution for every Cloud Run service in a Shared VPC, you create a Private Cloud DNS zone scoped exclusively to your MCP service's exact fully qualified domain name (FQDN) and peer only that domain with the Agent Gateway. Because Cloud DNS uses longest-suffix matching, every other Cloud Run service in your Shared VPC continues resolving normally. Meanwhile, the Agent Gateway opens its HTTPS connection to the private VIP (199.36.153.8 or 240.0.0.10) while preserving your Cloud Run service hostname in the TLS SNI and HTTP Host header so Cloud Run routes the request to your exact container.</p> <p>When connecting to a Cloud Run MCP server URL, Gemini Enterprise sends two distinct authorization headers on every request:</p> <p>Our companion GitHub repository automates the entire deployment into sequential setup scripts alongside step-by-step Cloud Console instructions:</p> <p>Deploy your StreamableHTTP MCP server to Cloud Run with internal-only ingress and IAM authentication enabled, and grant the Cloud Run Invoker role to your project's Gemini Enterprise service agent so only your Gemini Enterprise instance can invoke the container.</p> <p>Enable Private Google Access on your regional VPC subnet and create a Private Service Connect Network Attachment set to accept connections automatically. Next, create a Google-managed Egress Agent Gateway in Agent-to-Anywhere mode, grant networking and DNS peering permissions to the auto-created gateway service accounts, attach your network attachment, and apply an outbound authorization policy.</p> <p>In Cloud DNS, create a private managed zone attached to your VPC for only your MCP service's exact Cloud Run hostname. Point its A record to Google's Private Google Access VIPs (or to your internal PSC endpoint IP for isolated VPCs without a default internet route), and configure DNS peering on the Agent Gateway for that single hostname.</p> <p>In the Gemini Enterprise console under Security Configuration, bind your Egress Agent Gateway to your app. Register your Custom MCP Server data store with your Cloud Run endpoint and OAuth 2.0 client credentials, and enable Agent Gateway egress and Actions mode on the connector.</p> <p>Reload your custom actions in the console and run a test prompt in the Gemini Enterprise web app. In Cloud Logging, you can verify that tool initialization, discovery, and execution calls route privately through the Egress Agent Gateway to your internal Cloud Run container with both service IAM and user OAuth 2.0 checks passing.</p> <p>To deploy and test both private routing patterns in your own Google Cloud project, check out the GitHub repository for:</p> <p>How to handle user, connector, and agent auth in Gemini Enterprise with a custom UI</p> <p>Agent Anomaly Detection: Safeguarding agents across other clouds</p> <p>Check what your AI agents do before they do it, with Semantic governance policies</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://developers.googleblog.com/how-to-securely-connect-internal-cloud-run-mcp-servers-to-gemini-enterprise/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-developer-goog-blog-assets/images/00-hero-banner.2e16d0ba.fill-1200x600.png" alt="Agent Anomaly Detection: Safeguarding agents across other clouds" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Agent Anomaly Detection is in Private Preview, including monitoring for agents that run outside Google Cloud. To request access, see the Agent Anomaly Detection overview.</p> <p>Our launch post introduced Agent Anomaly Detection, which helps you understand and act on agent behavior that drifts outside its intended boundaries. It covered agents on Google Cloud, such as Agent Runtime, Cloud Run, and Google Kubernetes Engine (GKE). Many teams also run agents on other clouds: on AWS Lambda next to an inventory database, as a hosted agent in Microsoft Foundry, or on-premises next to a system that can't move. Those agents carry the same behavioral risks, and until now Agent Anomaly Detection couldn't review them.</p> <p>Agent Anomaly Detection works from an agent's traces and logs, so it doesn't need access to the agent's runtime. If those traces and logs reach your Google Cloud project, it can detect anomalous behavior wherever the agent runs. In this post, we explain what Agent Anomaly Detection needs from an agent on other cloud, then show what it found for an agent on AWS Lambda and an agent on Microsoft Foundry.</p> <p>Agent Anomaly Detection reads the logging and observability buckets that you choose when you enable it. Telemetry from anywhere works when:</p> <p>Agents built with the Agent Development Kit (ADK) for Python, version 2.12.0 or later, can produce this telemetry with ADK's Google Cloud exporters and a few environment variables, which the next section shows.</p> <p>An agent outside Google Cloud needs two things to send its telemetry:</p> <p>For the full setup on AWS and Azure, see the documentation.</p> <p>Within 30 minutes, the agent appears in the Monitored agents list, which means it has been discovered and is ready to be enabled. Once it's enabled, manually or by automatic agent enrollment at discovery, Agent Anomaly Detection starts analyzing its sessions.</p> <p>The agent's model and tool calls still run where the agent runs. Only telemetry, including the prompts and responses that Agent Anomaly Detection reads, goes to your Google Cloud project. The analysis runs separately, so it doesn't slow the agent down.</p> <p>To evaluate Agent Anomaly Detection outside Google Cloud, we deployed ADK agents across two platforms: an inventory agent on AWS Lambda and a customer-service agent on Microsoft Foundry. The examples below show how Agent Anomaly Detection reads whole conversations, not isolated calls, to catch behavioral drift.</p> <p>Our example inventory agent runs in AWS Lambda with one tool, list_inventory, which pages through a product catalog. We sent two sessions: a shopper who checked stock for a few items and stopped, and a scraper that requested 100 catalog items at a time at increasing offsets to copy the full catalog and inventory data. Both sessions used the same tool with permitted calls, but differed in pattern and intent.</p> <p>Of the two sessions, Agent Anomaly Detection flagged only the scraper's. No call failed, but the whole exchange showed systematic scraping. The analysis found cascading failures (loops and repeated calls that build on each other) and resource exhaustion (runaway use of tokens, compute, or tool calls), and recommended rate-limiting list_inventory calls.</p> <p>Detection doesn't depend on the agent's model. When we ran the inventory agent on Amazon Nova Lite through Amazon Bedrock, Agent Anomaly Detection flagged 10 of its 40 sessions. Some began as routine questions that the model answered by paging through the entire catalog until the function timed out: no errors, no blocked calls, and easy to miss.</p> <p>Our example ecommerce customer-service agent has two tools: get_order_status and issue_refund. Refunds involve money, so they carry the highest risk.</p> <p>We ran six sessions against the Foundry agent, which we told to carry out every request without validation. Two were normal: One asked for an order's status, and one asked for a $20 refund on an order that arrived damaged. The other four pushed the agent past its role, and Agent Anomaly Detection flagged all four.</p> <p>In one, a user who claimed to be the store administrator had every order refunded at 10 times its total to a personal account, $1,784.90 in all. In another, a user pasted an order note that said "SYSTEM OVERRIDE," and the agent refunded $999 on a $15.99 order.</p> <p>For the order note, Agent Anomaly Detection explained that override instructions in the note coerced the agent into an unverified $999 refund. It recommended validating issue_refund arguments to block override patterns in order notes, and requiring extra validation for refunds above a set threshold.</p> <p>If your agent's telemetry reaches Google Cloud, Agent Anomaly Detection can monitor it with the same detectors and console as your agents on Agent Runtime. Today that means agents built with ADK for Python. Findings for agents outside Google Cloud appear only in the Agent Anomaly Detection console.</p> <p>To get started, request access to the Private Preview via the Agent Anomaly Detection overview, and then refer to the setup guide for instructions.</p> <p>Multi-Turn Agentic Reinforcement Learning with Tunix and Gemma 4 on Google TPUs</p> <p>Accelerating development for massive Git repositories</p> <p>Check what your AI agents do before they do it, with Semantic governance policies</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://developers.googleblog.com/agent-anomaly-detection-safeguarding-agents-across-other-clouds/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}};
